DIAGNOSTIC ACCURACY OF MRI IN DETECTING ROTATOR CUFF TEARS TAKING INDIRECT MR ARTHROGRAPHY AS GOLD STANDARD

DR. MARIA MURTAZA , DR MUHAMMAD AQEEL BABRI , DR. AFAF ARIF , DR. HASSAN RASHID , — 2026-12-15 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.18464498

Background: Rotator cuff tears are a common cause of shoulder pain and functional limitation. Accurate diagnosis is essential for appropriate management. While indirect MR arthrography (MRA) is considered the gold standard, plain MRI is widely used due to its non-invasive nature. Objectives: To determine the diagnostic accuracy of plain MRI in detecting rotator cuff tears, using indirect MR arthrography as the reference standard, and to evaluate its performance across different patient subgroups. Study Design & Setting: Cross-sectional validated study conducted at the Department of Radiology, Ittefaq Hospital Trust, Lahore from July 2025 to October 2025. Methodology: A total of 125 patients aged 2070 years with clinical suspicion of rotator cuff tear were enrolled using consecutive sampling. All patients underwent plain MRI followed by indirect MRA in the same imaging session. Images were independently interpreted by two experienced radiologists blinded to each others findings. Diagnostic performance of plain MRI was assessed using 2×2 contingency tables, calculating sensitivity, specificity, positive predictive value (PPV), negative predictive value (NPV), and overall accuracy. Stratification was performed by age, gender, and duration of symptoms. Results: Plain MRI detected 76/125 tears compared to 82/125 on indirect MRA. Overall sensitivity and specificity were 86.6% and 88.4%, respectively, with a PPV of 93.4%, NPV of 77.6%, and overall accuracy of 87.2%. Stratified analysis demonstrated high diagnostic performance across age, gender, and symptom duration subgroups, with statistically significant differences (p < 0.001). Conclusion: Plain MRI shows high diagnostic accuracy for detecting rotator cuff tears and may serve as a reliable non-invasive imaging modality, though indirect MRA remains the gold standard.

A Systemic Observational Framework Integrating Physiotherapy-Derived Functional, Physiological, And Behavioral Indicators In Predicting Pharmacological Response

Dr. Kanchan Kholiya, Dr. Shalini dixit, Ram Sihag (PT), Dr. Samarpita Senapati (PT), Dr. Harish Sharma (PT), Dr. Gurpreet Kaur (PT)), Dr. Harshika Gupta (PT). — 2026-11-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.18309329

Background: Inter-individual variability in pharmacological response remains a persistent challenge in clinical rehabilitation. While pharmacokinetic and molecular mechanisms are well established, they account for only a proportion of observed outcome variability. Functional performance, physiological regulation, and behavioral engagement—core domains of physiotherapy—are increasingly recognized as influential determinants of therapeutic response. Aim: To develop and describe a systemic observational framework integrating physiotherapy-derived functional, physiological, and behavioral indicators with pharmacological response, supported by descriptive statistical reasoning. Methodology: A conceptual observational methodology was adopted. Peer-reviewed literature was synthesized, and commonly reported trends, proportions, and associations were interpreted using descriptive and inferential statistical logic (e.g., variability, correlation strength, explained variance), without primary data collection. Results: Evidence across rehabilitation and pharmacological literature consistently indicates that functional and behavioral variables account for a substantial proportion of outcome variability unexplained by pharmacological parameters alone. Descriptive trends suggest moderate-to-strong associations between autonomic regulation, movement efficiency, behavioral engagement, and therapeutic stability. Conclusion: Incorporating physiotherapy-derived indicators into pharmacological interpretation enhances explanatory power and clinical relevance. A systemic, statistically informed observational framework supports personalized, interdisciplinary healthcare.

DIAGNOSTIC ACCURACY OF ULTRA-LOW-DOSE CT VS. STANDARD-DOSE CT FOR PULMONARY NODULE DETECTION: A SYSTEMATIC REVIEW AND META-ANALYSIS

KHALED BONNA , RASHA GALAL ELSAFTY , NORA EID ALANAZI , RANIN JABER M ALMAJRASHI AHLAM ALI Y ASIRI , WALEED MUFAREH ALSHAHRANI , SOMAYYAH SOHAIL MITHA , SHOUG NASSER ALKHAMMASH , BANDAR SAAD ALSHREEF — 2026-11-05 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.18243485

Background: Standard dose computed tomography (CT) is a primary modality for detecting pulmonary nodules (PNs), but the health risk from cumulative radiation exposure has prompted the emergence of ultra-low-dose CT (ULDCT) as an alternative to reduce this dose, because its diagnostic accuracy remains variable, this systematic review and meta-analysis aims to synthesize the existing evidence to determine the diagnostic accuracy and effective radiation dose of ULDCT for pulmonary nodule detection. Methods: Following PRISMA guidelines, a systematic search was conducted in PubMed, Cochrane Library, and Ovid MEDLINE for original research published between 2015 and 2025 that evaluated the diagnostic accuracy of ULDCT for PN detection, for which a meta-analysis using random-effects models was performed to calculate the pooled sensitivity for nodule detection and the pooled mean effective radiation dose, with a subgroup analysis conducted to assess the impact of different reconstruction algorithms. Results: Forty-two studies comprising 15,792 patients were included, from which a meta-analysis of 11 studies demonstrated a pooled sensitivity of 91.9% (95% CI: 84.6%–97.1%) for PN detection with heterogeneity (I²=93.0%), and a pooled mean effective dose from 12 studies of 0.22 mSv (95% CI: 0.11–0.33 mSv), a level comparable to standard chest radiography, while subgroup analysis revealed a trend towards higher sensitivity with advanced reconstruction algorithms (96.7%) compared to standard iterative reconstruction (87.9%) that was not statistically significant (p=0.091), and a narrative synthesis confirmed ULDCT's accuracy for solid nodules but identified reduced sensitivity for subsolid, ground-glass, and small (<6 mm) nodules, with performance limited in patients with a high body mass index (BMI). Conclusion: ULDCT combined with advanced reconstruction techniques offers high diagnostic accuracy for the detection of solid pulmonary nodules and reduces radiation exposure to levels approaching that of a chest X-ray, but its utility is limited for detecting subsolid and small nodules and in obese patients, making protocol standardization and patient-specific considerations essential to optimize its clinical implementation.

SPATIAL TUMOR ECOSYSTEMS: Architecture, Plasticity, and Therapeutic Vulnerabilities in Diffuse Midline Glioma

Raphaël Vinz Ulysse Collot, Anne C. Rios, Pierre A. J. T. Robe — 2026-11-04 — openalex

DOI : —

Diffuse midline glioma (DMG) is a devastating pediatric brain cancer with an almost universally fatal prognosis. Despite detailed knowledge of its genetic and epigenetic drivers, durable therapeutic responses remain rare. This thesis investigates whether the spatial organization of tumor and immune cells helps explain the gap between molecular understanding and clinical progress. We propose that DMG functions as a spatially organized, adaptive ecosystem comprising distinct but interdependent cellular niches. Embedded within the developing brain, these communities coexist and may compensate for one another under therapeutic pressure. Understanding their organization and interactions could reveal vulnerabilities overlooked by approaches targeting individual pathways or cell types. In Chapter 2, we introduce mLSR-3D, a protocol for large-scale three-dimensional imaging of intact tissue. By preserving spatial relationships lost during tissue dissociation, this approach reveals patterns of immune infiltration and tumor organization that are not readily captured by conventional two-dimensional imaging or dissociative analyses. In Chapter 3, we identify the IGSF11-VISTA checkpoint axis as a mechanism through which DMG maintains immune quiescence. Tumor cells engage this pathway to preserve microglia, the brain’s resident immune cells, in a homeostatic, low-activation state, limiting spontaneous antitumor immunity. Disrupting this axis in preclinical models reactivates microglial effector functions and induces tumor regression. These findings demonstrate that microglia retain antitumor potential and suggest that DMG exploits regulatory mechanisms of developmental immune homeostasis to evade immune attack. Chapter 4 provides a framework for multi-omics profiling of sequential tissue sections, enabling integrated analysis of transcriptomic, proteomic, and spatial features within the same tumors. In Chapter 5, we develop pHGGmap, a spatially informed single-cell atlas of pediatric high-grade gliomas integrating datasets from more than 100 patients. This resource identifies recurrent tumor and immune cell states and supports a model of DMG organized around three spatial niches: an astro-microglial niche containing more differentiated tumor cells and homeostatic microglia; a tumor-reactive niche shaped by hypoxia and inflammation and containing immunosuppressive macrophages; and a persistent, stem-enriched niche associated with immunosuppressive myeloid cells and tumor invasion. Although their relative abundance changes following therapy, the overall ecosystem architecture remains remarkably stable, potentially contributing to treatment resistance. In Chapter 6, we reconsider immune checkpoint inhibition, oncolytic virotherapy, and CAR T-cell therapy through this ecosystem framework. Beyond molecular resistance and antigen escape, incomplete disruption of tumor niches and compensatory interactions may allow the disease to persist. Durable control may therefore require combinations that target multiple niches simultaneously and adapt to changes in the tumor over time. Together, this thesis defines architectural and regulatory principles governing tumor-immune interactions in DMG. It highlights both the resilience of spatially organized tumor communities and the therapeutic potential of reactivating brain-resident immunity, providing a foundation for ecosystem-informed strategies aimed at lasting disease control.

Pushing boundaries in Pompe disease:On optimal dosing, home-based enzyme replacement therapy, and the impact of antidrug antibodies

Imke A. M. Ditters — 2026-11-02 — openalex

DOI : —

Pompe disease is a rare lysosomal storage disease in which glycogen accumulates due to a deficiency of α-glucosidase. In the Netherlands, we have over 25 years of experience with Enzyme Replacement Therapy (ERT) for Pompe disease, with ERT having fundamentally changed the clinical landscape. As patients now survive longer, two key challenges have become apparent: the considerable variability in treatment response and substantial and lifelong burden associated with ERT. The optimal dosage of ERT has been a long-standing debate. In part A, chapter 2, it was demonstrated in the largest international classic infantile cohort to date that patients treated with the high ERT dosage of 40 mg/kg per week have significantly improved survival when compared with patients treated with the standard recommended ERT dosage of 20 mg/kg every other week. In addition, a clinically meaningful difference in walking ability was found between patient groups. When rhGAA was approved for treatment of Pompe disease, home infusions were not included in the summary of product characteristics (SmPC). Thus, in many countries, hospital infusions remain the standard treatment. In the Netherlands, however, home therapy for Pompe disease has been implemented since 2008. In part B of this thesis, we focus on the safety and feasibility of home-based ERT. The experience with home infusion using the Dutch protocol is described. Chapter 3 demonstrated, based on the analysis of 18,380 infusions in late-onset Pompe disease (LOPD) patients, that ERT with alglucosidase alfa can safely be administered at home. Only a few IARs occurred during ERT with rhGAA, most of them being mild and not necessitating clinical intervention. In Chapter 4, the analysis of 11,898 infusions with rhGAA in children with Pompe disease demonstrated that most IARs were mild. Classic infantile patients were more likely to develop IARs, but home treatment is feasible in all patient groups provided the appropriate protocols and infrastructure are in place. Safety of home-based ERT was further confirmed based on patient questionnaires in Chapter 5. With the majority of Pompe patients developing IgG antibody titres, the effect of anti-rhGAA antibodies on treatment efficacy was studied in part C. In Chapter 6, the review on anti-rhGAA antibody formation in late-onset Pompe disease patients, no discernible effect was found of anti-rhGAA IgG antibodies on treatment response among most patients. In some patients a clinical decline related to (possible) interference of anti-rhGAA antibodies was described. Similarly, in Chapter 7, anti-rhGAA antibodies did not significantly impact clinical outcomes at group level in late-onset Pompe disease patients. However, their (neutralising) effect can negatively impact disease course in individual patients and high sustained antibody titres are associated with an increased risk of IARs. In Chapter 8, no statistically significant correlation was observed between peak antibody titres and IARs in childhood-onset patients, although the majority of IARs occurred in patients with high titres. The insights gained from this thesis provide guidance towards optimal ERT dosing in classic infantile Pompe disease, demonstrate that ERT can be safely administered at home in both patients with classic infantile and late-onset Pompe disease, provide protocols to do so, and offer insight into the effects of anti-rhGAA antibodies on treatment efficacy and outcome. This thesis paves the way to further improve treatment and effectively implement next-generation ERTs for Pompe disease, both at home and in hospital.

Towards closing gaps in livestock physiologically-based kinetic modeling: Machine learning prediction of total plasma clearance and tissue-plasma partition coefficients, and integration of growth in a physiologically-based kinetic model with a case study in PFOS

David Inauen, Institute for Risk Assesment Sciences, Ronette Gehring, Jessy Hendriks, Leonie Lautz — 2026-11-02 — openalex

DOI : —

Physiologically-based kinetic (PBK) models simulate the absorption, distribution, metabolism, and excretion (ADME) of chemicals in the body. A PBK model consists of multiple compartments, representing various bodily tissues, each involving tissue-specific properties and processes. In this thesis, the focus lies on toxicological PBK modeling for livestock species. A PBK model requires a certain number of input parameters, which are often unavailable for livestock species; alternative approaches to parameter procurement need to be found. Part one of the thesis consists of in silico approaches for predicting clearance and tissue partitioning using machine learning (ML). The ML models were based on molecular information, and anatomical/physiological data of pre-clinical and livestock species, such as body weight or tissue composition. The ML predictions were better than those from legacy approaches such as allometric scaling or mechanistic models. Toxicological assessment in livestock often involves long-term exposure scenarios and/or chemicals with long half-lives. Furthermore, livestock experience rapid growth during the juvenile phase, resulting in changes in anatomy and physiology throughout most of their lifespan, with implications for the ADME. In part two of the thesis, growth curves for different livestock species were estimated for implementation in PBK models. A case study of perfluorooctanesulfonic acid exposure in livestock was then conducted, using the growth-integrated PBK model. Inclusion of growth increased prediction accuracy of concentrations in both plasma and tissues. For long-term exposures, and/or juvenile animals, and/or chemicals with long half lives, growth should be accounted for in livestock PBK models.

The Relationship Between Political Affiliation, Perceptions of Science and Scientists, and Perceived Safety and Effectiveness of the MMR, Flu, and COVID-19 Vaccines: United States, 2024.

Dror Walter, Yotam Ophir, Patrick E. Jamieson, Kathleen Hall-Jamieson — 2026-11-01 — openalex

Sociopolitical crisis and non-state schooling in Jordan

Rensimer, Lee, Epstein, Andrew — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : —

Non-state schools, a broad category of educational providers funded, owned or managed by private, philanthropic, or intergovernmental organizations, are increasingly seen by the global donor community as potential partners in meeting national and international educational development goals. The growing presence of non-state schools in the Levant region, particularly Lebanon, Jordan and the Occupied Palestinian Territories, acutely reflects this trend; every year, more parents choose to send their children to non-state schools in these countries. While these developments have arguably led to increased access to education at all levels, the heavier reliance on non-state schools, especially for-profit private providers, creates new challenges. The departure of higher performing pupils from the public sector weakens the system overall due to peer effects. Furthermore, non-state schools can exit the market when facing financial constraints like during economic or health crises, in turn causing mass inflows of pupils back into an already stressed state school system. This chapter examines the non-state school profile of the Jordanian education system before and during the COVID-19 pandemic, looking at the differential impacts of the pandemic on state and non-state school enrollment. Drawing on a suite of statistical data on educational provision and consumption in Jordan, it follows the sharp withdrawal of private providers in 2020 and increase in state school enrollments, illustrating the underlying risk and limited resilience of non-state educational provision.

Caloric restriction attenuates age-related liver and kidney alterations in mice: Histological evidence.

Carlos Roberto Montes-de-Oca-Saucedo, Sheila Adela Villa‐Cedillo, Cristina Sarahi Ríos-Vazquez, Eduardo A Álvarez-Tostado-Ojeda, Mario Murguía‐Pérez — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.14670/hh-25-065

OBJECTIVE: This study evaluated the histological impact of long-term caloric restriction (CR) on liver and kidney aging, assessing fibrosis, metabolic overload, and senescence-associated readouts. METHODS: , and acetylated p53 (Lys382) in both organs, and β-galactosidase immunoreactivity (IHC-DAB) in liver. Sections underwent morphometric quantification and statistical analysis. RESULTS: Aged SD mice exhibited liver injury, increased PAS positivity, and collagen deposition, whereas CR attenuated these changes. The SD 16M group displayed the highest PAS positivity, while CR limited glycogen accumulation and preserved lobular architecture. In the kidney, SD animals developed tubular degeneration and architectural disruption; CR preserved renal structure and reduced tubular damage. CR was associated with higher SIRT1 immunoreactivity and lower acetylated p53, consistent with preserved SIRT1-linked deacetylation status. p16-positive cells increased with age under SD and were reduced by CR, though not fully normalized at 16 months. β-galactosidase immunoreactivity was evident in aged SD hepatocytes but minimal in CR groups. CONCLUSION: Long-term CR preserves hepatic and renal histoarchitecture during aging by limiting metabolic overload, fibrotic remodeling, and senescence, accompanied by a sustained SIRT1/acetyl-p53 profile consistent with reduced p53 activation.

The Pathway to Achieving Competitive Advantage in Banking: Development and Testing of a Five-Layer Hierarchical Model

Mahdi Sharaf Tadavani, Roohalah Negahdari Nia — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : —

The objective of this study was to develop and empirically test a five-layer hierarchical model explaining the sequential pathway through which banks achieve sustainable competitive advantage. This applied research adopted a descriptive–correlational design with an organizational level of analysis. The statistical population consisted of senior and middle managers of public and private banks, from which a sample of 400 managers was selected using simple random sampling. Data were collected using a researcher-developed questionnaire including 25 items representing 5 latent constructs, with 5 items per construct, measured on a 5-point Likert scale. Content validity was confirmed through expert evaluation, and reliability was assessed using Cronbach’s alpha, composite reliability, and average variance extracted, all exceeding recommended thresholds. Data analysis was conducted using Partial Least Squares Structural Equation Modeling (PLS-SEM), and bank ownership type (public vs. private) was included as a control variable in the structural model. The results showed that organizational structure and systems had a significant positive effect on human capital and organizational culture (β = 0.79, p < 0.01) and on stakeholder quality and management (β = 0.46, p < 0.01), but no significant effect on ethical and adaptive leadership (p > 0.05). Human capital and organizational culture had a strong positive effect on ethical and adaptive leadership (β = 0.84, p < 0.01), which in turn significantly influenced stakeholder quality and management (β = 0.49, p < 0.01). Stakeholder quality and management exerted a very strong effect on strategic growth and competitive advantage (β = 0.89, p < 0.01). The coefficients of determination ranged from R² = 0.63 to R² = 0.78, indicating high explanatory power. The findings confirm that competitive advantage in banking is achieved through a structured, multi-layer process in which organizational structure, human capital, leadership, and stakeholder management interact sequentially to drive strategic growth.

The Effectiveness of Cognitive Behavioral Therapy on Resilience and Marital Adjustment among Couples with Marital Conflicts

Hana Faraji, Eshagh Samkhaniani, Zeynab Naghipour, Maedeh Ghanbari, Elahe Ebrahimi Alavige — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : —

Objective: The present study aimed to determine the effectiveness of cognitive behavioral therapy on resilience and marital adjustment among couples experiencing marital conflicts. Methods and Materials: The present study employed a quasi-experimental method with a pretest–posttest design and a control group. The statistical population consisted of couples with marital conflicts who referred to counseling centers in Tehran during 2025. Using convenience sampling, 28 participants were selected and randomly assigned into two groups, including an experimental group (n = 14) and a control group (n = 14). Data collection instruments included the Connor–Davidson Resilience Scale and the Spanier Dyadic Adjustment Scale. The experimental group participated in eight 2-hour sessions of cognitive behavioral therapy, while the control group received no intervention. Data were analyzed using descriptive statistics and analysis of covariance in SPSS software. Findings: The findings indicated that cognitive behavioral therapy had a significant effect on both resilience and marital adjustment among couples with marital conflicts. The results of covariance analysis demonstrated that the effects of time, group, and the interaction between time and group were statistically significant for resilience and marital adjustment at the p < .001 level. Participants in the experimental group showed significantly higher posttest scores in resilience and marital adjustment compared to the control group, indicating the effectiveness of the intervention in improving psychological resilience and relational functioning. Conclusion: The results of the present study demonstrated that cognitive behavioral therapy is an effective intervention for improving resilience and marital adjustment among couples experiencing marital conflicts. By modifying maladaptive cognitions, enhancing emotional regulation, and improving communication and coping skills, cognitive behavioral therapy can strengthen couples’ psychological adaptability and relational quality. Therefore, CBT-based interventions may be beneficial in counseling and therapeutic settings for reducing marital distress and promoting healthier interpersonal relationships among couples.

Histopathological and molecular mechanisms of multi-organ damage in heat stroke: Endothelial failure, immune crosstalk, and regulated cell death pathways.

Myoung Cheol Shin, Bo Hyun Jung, Joongbum Moon, Ji Hyeon Ahn, Jun Hwi Cho — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.14670/hh-25-076

Heat stroke is a life-threatening condition characterized by severe hyperthermia accompanied by central nervous system dysfunction and progressive multi-organ injury. Although rapid cooling remains the cornerstone of clinical management, accumulating evidence indicates that normalization of core body temperature alone does not fully prevent delayed organ damage. Experimental and clinical studies increasingly demonstrate that heat stroke-induced pathology extends beyond acute thermal injury and involves sustained endothelial dysfunction, coagulation imbalance, sterile inflammation, mitochondrial failure, and regulated cell death pathways. Histopathological analyses across multiple organs, including the brain, liver, kidneys, and intestine, reveal persistent microvascular injury, tissue edema, microthrombi formation, inflammatory infiltration, and cellular degeneration following the hyperthermic phase. These tissue-level alterations are closely linked to molecular mechanisms such as endothelial glycocalyx disruption, inflammasome activation, mitochondrial dysfunction, and dysregulated immune-coagulation crosstalk. Importantly, these interconnected processes form a self-amplifying injury network that contributes to delayed neurological deterioration and systemic organ failure despite effective cooling. This review integrates histopathological and molecular evidence from experimental models and human studies to provide a systems-level framework of post-hyperthermic injury in heat stroke. By linking tissue pathology with underlying mechanisms and translational therapeutic targets, this article highlights why cooling alone is insufficient and emphasizes the need for multimodal, mechanism-based interventions to mitigate delayed organ injury and improve clinical outcomes.

Presenting a Model of Organizational Pride in Tehran Municipality: A Qualitative Study

Abbas Pazokiy, Farshad Hajalian, Shahram Bandpey, Mansoreh Moradi Haghighi — 2026-11-01 — openalex

DOI : —

The present study aimed to develop and explain a qualitative model of organizational pride in Tehran Municipality by identifying its underlying dimensions, components, and thematic structure from the perspectives of academic experts, organizational specialists, and municipal managers. This study employed a qualitative research design using thematic analysis. The statistical population consisted of university faculty members in the fields of public administration, human resource management, and organizational behavior, experienced organizational experts, and senior managers of Tehran Municipality. Participants were selected through purposive sampling followed by snowball sampling. Data collection continued until theoretical saturation was achieved. Saturation was reached after the twelfth interview, although three additional interviews were conducted to ensure completeness, resulting in a final sample of fifteen participants. Data were collected through semi-structured, in-depth interviews lasting between 60 and 120 minutes. Interviews were transcribed verbatim and analyzed using thematic network analysis with the support of MAXQDA 2024 software. To enhance trustworthiness, member checking, peer review, participant feedback, and expert evaluation procedures were employed. Intercoder reliability was assessed using Holsti’s coefficient, which indicated a high level of agreement. The analysis revealed that organizational pride in Tehran Municipality is a multidimensional construct composed of three overarching themes, twelve organizing themes, and one hundred twenty basic themes. The first overarching theme, identity and belonging pride, encompassed deep organizational belonging, pride in individual and team development, pride in workplace and welfare, security and reward-based validation, and pride in cultural and traditional heritage. The second overarching theme, mission-oriented and value-based pride, included social impact competence, ethical credibility and organizational transparency, and respect for leadership and senior management. The third overarching theme, efficiency and performance pride, comprised organizational credibility and position, competence in innovation and performance achievements, credibility of processes and systems, and pride in qualitative and practical achievements. The findings indicated that organizational pride is formed through the interaction of employees’ emotional attachment to the organization, commitment to meaningful public service and organizational values, and perceptions of organizational effectiveness, innovation, and social reputation. The findings demonstrate that organizational pride is a comprehensive and multidimensional phenomenon that extends beyond emotional attachment and encompasses identity, values, social contribution, organizational reputation, leadership, innovation, and performance. In the context of Tehran Municipality, employees’ pride is strengthened when they experience a strong sense of belonging, perceive their work as socially meaningful, trust organizational leadership and ethical practices, and recognize organizational competence and achievements. The proposed model provides a comprehensive framework for understanding organizational pride in public-sector organizations and offers practical insights for managers seeking to enhance employee engagement, commitment, citizenship behavior, and organizational effectiveness through the development of pride-enhancing organizational environments.

Exposure Geography Determines Phenotype in Lyme Borreliosis: A Case of Imported Early Localized Disease

Y. Ivanova, Nina Yancheva — 2026-10-25 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23259040

INTRODUCTION: Lyme borreliosis is the northern hemisphere's commonest tick-borne infection: ~476,000 US patients are diagnosed and treated annually, while European surveillance records ~129,000 reported cases. Untreated, it disseminates, causing arthritis, neuroborreliosis, carditis, and, in Europe, acrodermatitis chronica atrophicans. Phenotype differs between continents, where distinct Borrelia genospecies predominate, so travel-associated cases risk misinterpretation. We report imported early Lyme borreliosis diagnosed clinically in Bulgaria. METHODS: Single-patient case from the Emergency Department of Infectious Disease Hospital in Sofia, Bulgaria, July 2026. Diagnosis rested on history and examination alone, according to current international recommendations (IDSA/AAN/ACR), which advise treating erythema migrans without serological confirmation. Published comparative cohort data informed interpretation. RESULTS: A 55-year-old woman from rural, deer-abundant Connecticut developed fever, chills, myalgia and arthralgia over several days while on vacation in Bulgaria, followed by an 8 cm expanding annular rash on her back. No tick bite was recalled. Her stay of under three days was shorter than the minimum incubation period to erythema migrans, placing acquisition pre-travel. Systemic symptoms accompany 69% of US erythema migrans versus 37-38% in Europe, where central clearing and larger lesions predominate. Doxycycline 100 mg twice daily was started immediately. No blood tests were obtained; locally non-endemic Connecticut co-infections were not considered. CONCLUSION: Exposure geography determines the expected phenotype. Erythema migrans warrants immediate treatment without serology, which is insensitive early and genospecies-dependent. Omitting blood tests was a limitation: travel history should prompt screening for anaplasmosis and babesiosis. Multivalent OspA vaccines spanning both continents’ serotypes may protect cross-boundary travellers. Emergence of flaviviruses

Christova Iva — 2026-10-24 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23257444

Emergence of viruses belonging to family Flaviviridae is a serious global health problem. Due to climate change and increased global transport, Aedes mosquitoes and ticks that serve as vectors of the flaviviruses are expanding their range. As a consequence, typical tropical diseases emerge in temperate latitudes of Europe including Bulgaria. Emergences of West Nile virus and dengue virus along with chikungunya virus (genus Alphavirus, Togaviridae family) are most pronounced. West Nile virus is the most widespread flavivirus in Europe. It is transmitted by common mosquitoes (Culex) and affects dozens of European countries, including all Balkan countries. About 0.5-1% of the infected patients clinically manifest a life-threatening encephalitis. Dengue is traditionally a tropical disease. However, due to its high levels of transmission, it can cause outbreaks in European countries. Local transmission leading to outbreaks is regularly recorded in southern European countries such as Spain, France, and Italy. The reason is the wide distribution of the Asian tiger mosquito (Aedes albopictus). In addition, two other flavivirus infections continue to cause serious health threats: yellow fever and zika. Yellow fever causes serious concern with new outbreaks in South America (e.g. Colombia) and Africa, requiring strict international control. Zika virus continues to circulate actively in the Americas and Southeast Asia, with isolated cases also observed in Europe. Finally, a tick-borne flavivirus infection expands its range of distribution. Tick-borne encephalitis is common in forested areas of Central and Northern Europe. As the climate warms, endemic areas are moving further. The main three factors for expansion of the flaviviruses are climate change, urbanization, and travel or trade. Milder winters and hotter summers allow mosquitoes to survive more easily in new regions and extend their active period. Artificial containers of water in urban environments are the perfect breeding ground for tiger mosquitoes. Infected travelers or goods carry viruses across continents in hours. Prevention is based on personal protection: use of repellents, wearing long clothing in risk areas, and installing window screens; urban disinfestation: regular spraying against larvae and adults, removal of standing water, emptying water containers around homes every few days to prevent egg laying; and vaccination: effective vaccines are available against dengue, yellow fever and tick-borne encephalitis Abstract code: KL-01Presentation type: Key lecturePresenting author: Prof. Iva ChristovaPresenting-author country (as published): BulgariaContaining publication: Abstract Book: 4th Annual BAVBD MeetingPrinted page: 11ISBN of containing book: 978-86-909710-0-84th Annual BAVBD Meeting24-25 October 2026Park Hotel Moskva, Sofia, BulgariaPublisher: Balkan Association for Vector-Borne Diseases (BAVBD)Website: www.bavbd.org

Signal and System Analysis of Wireless Attenuation in a Steel Enclosed Ultrafiltration Container: A Standards Based Fourier Laplace Framework

Stanley Ifeanyi Obiadika, Euler C. T. Macêdo, Juan M. M. Villanueva, Raimundo C. S. Freire — 2026-10-20 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23042334

Reliable wireless connectivity is critical for monitoring and remotely operating modern water treatment infrastructure. This paper offers a signals and systems interpretation of a field study at a containerized ultrafiltration plant of Companhia de Água e Esgotos da Paraíba (CAGEPA) state water and sewage utility, in João Pessoa, Brazil, where staff reported unstable internet access inside a steel shipping container. Received Signal Strength Indicator (RSSI) and Signal to Noise Ratio (SNR) were measured with a handheld spectrum analyzer at four points of increasing depth into the container, revealing a monotonic decline from -55 dBm/28 dB (outside) to -85 dBm/10 dB (far end). Rather than treating this decline as a purely empirical curve, we model the container as a linear time invariant (LTI) system acting on the transmitted RF signal and apply the theoretical machinery of Digital Signal Processing (DSP): the Fourier transform for frequency domain characterization, the Laplace transform for system/transfer function analysis, sampling theory for justifying the discretized instrument readings, and normalization (dBm, SNR, min max, z-score) for standardizing heterogeneous measurements. The resulting framework explains the observed attenuation as a frequency dependent shielding effect consistent with the skin effect behavior of conductive enclosures, and provides a methodological bridge between classical DSP theory and practical RF site survey engineering.

Naar verbeterde DNA-profilering bij forensisch onderzoek naar seksueel geweld: Ontwikkeling van nieuwe strategieën voor de scheiding van zaadcellen

Kristina Fokias — 2026-10-15 — openalex

DOI : —

Sexual violence is a global issue, disproportionally affecting women and girls with a profound impact on the physical, psychological, and social health of victims. With the large majority of the perpetrators being male, forensic investigations of reported rape cases typically focus on identifying the suspect through the recovery of a sperm-derived DNA profile from biological samples collected from the victim. However, obtaining a single-source DNA profile remains highly challenging when biological traces contain cellular material from multiple contributors, as they result in DNA mixtures that become increasingly difficult to interpret as the number of contributors rises. Moreover, the abundance of cellular material from the victim relative to that of the suspect often leads to the male DNA profile to be obscured. Conventional sperm cell enrichment methods often fail in samples with low sperm counts, which are frequently encountered in delayed-reported cases, while uninterpretable mixed DNA profiles are often obtained from samples containing sperm cells from multiple contributors, such as in multi-perpetrator rapes. Current procedures are further often time-consuming, labour-intensive, and prone to operator variability and contamination due to the many steps involved. The overarching goal of this PhD project was to address these challenges by introducing validated cell sorting and microfluidic strategies that improve the recovery of sperm-derived DNA under challenging conditions. Fluorescence-activated cell sorting (FACS) enables the detection and isolation of individual cells from mixed populations based on phenotypic markers. Although this powerful technology is strongly embedded in clinical research, it has only been explored to a limited extent in forensic sciences. The first part of this work therefore aimed to establish and systematically validate a FACS-based methodology for the separation of sperm cells from vaginal swab samples. This method consistently outperformed conventional methods in terms of sensitivity and sperm DNA purity, while the ability to obtain full male DNA profiles from samples collected 5 days after sexual intercourse emphasises its utility in delayed-reported rape cases. ISO/IEC 17025 accreditation was granted for this procedure, which was subsequently implemented in the UZ Leuven Forensic Genetics' Laboratory for sexual assault casework analysis. In the next phase, this methodology was leveraged for the deconvolution of multi-sperm contributor samples by means of single-cell analysis. An interpretational framework was applied wherein single-cell profiles were clustered based on reciprocal similarity, followed by subsequent probabilistic genotyping to infer designated donor contributions. Following this method, donors could be identified in samples comprising haploid profiles from up to 4 contributors, demonstrating its potential in the resolution of multi-offender cases. Motivated by the limitations of both conventional and cell-sorting approaches, the second part of this dissertation explored the potential of microfluidic technologies for sperm cell enrichment. A droplet-based microfluidic platform was developed, enabling the encapsulation, detection, and sorting of sperm cells in water-in-oil droplets. This method demonstrated high enrichment efficiency in semen samples and was further validated using mock biological mixtures and postcoital samples. Although further optimisation is required to enhance robustness, this proof-of-concept study represents the first demonstration of integrating droplet microfluidics into forensic workflows for sexual assault sample processing. In parallel, an alternative microfluidic strategy is under development to address limitations related to cost-efficiency and workload by implementing differential lysis on-chip. This platform aims to minimise operator intervention, reduce processing time, and improve standardisation across analyses. The present work provides foundational insights into bulk lysis protocols and microfluidic mixing strategies to support the continued development of this system. Collectively, this work offers new perspectives on highly sensitive, scalable, and/or cost-effective methods for the analysis of sperm-containing samples collected in sexual assault casework.

DNA damage induces long range changes to duplex structure—a non-protein start to damage detection?

S.E. Fountain, M.A.S. Abdelhamid, D.M. Williams, T.D. Craggs — 2026-10-14 — openalex

DOI : —

DNA-binding proteins must quickly locate specific sites on DNA to enable replication, repair, and transcription. While sequence-specific recognition is well understood, the physical basis of structure-specific recognition remains unclear, limiting our understanding of DNA damage repair. Proteins must distinguish damaged sites within largely undamaged DNA; however, studying this is challenging due to DNA’s dynamic nature. We hypothesized that DNA damage causes changes in DNA structure, signalling protein recruitment. Using confocal single-molecule FRET, we analysed five DNA duplexes containing modifications: ribonucleotide, 8-oxoguanine, an abasic site, a nick, and a gap, which are all involved in the base excision repair pathway. Each construct was measured with nine dye pairs in triplicate to capture changes in bending, twisting, and stretching. An automated analysis pipeline processed 162 measurements, enabling rigorous statistical comparisons. All modifications altered FRET efficiencies compared to undamaged DNA, including the subtlest change: a single oxygen difference (ribo- versus deoxyribonucleotide). Abasic sites, nicks, and gaps had the greatest effects. These findings provide direct evidence that DNA damage affects duplex structure and dynamics beyond the lesion site, suggesting DNA flexibility changes may act as an early signal for repair protein recruitment.

Clinic White Paper: USD Tele-Mental Health Training Clinic

Nicholas Boyd, Lauren Bruaski — 2026-10-13 — openalex

DOI : —

California is home to over 1.8 million veterans and 18,000 National Guard members, representing one of the largest military-connected populations in the United States. Despite substantial demand, over 50% of veterans experiencing mental health symptoms do not seek treatment, while those seeking care within federal systems frequently face significant wait times. This academic white paper evaluates the operational framework, clinical effectiveness, and workforce expansion model of the University of San Diego (USD) Tele-Mental Health Training Clinic – Veterans Initiative. Utilizing a secure, HIPAA-compliant telehealth model paired with a tiered supervision structure, the clinic provides evidence-based psychotherapies (EBPs) at no cost to veterans, National Guard service members, and their families across California. Since launching clinical services in March 2025, the clinic has completed nearly 6,000 clinical hours across nearly 400 clients. Longitudinal psychometric outcome monitoring using validated tools demonstrates statistically and clinically significant reductions in depression (PHQ-9), anxiety (GAD-7), and post-traumatic stress disorder (PCL-5), paired with high client retention and Net Promoter Scores in the “Excellent” range. This paper presents the clinic's clinical methodology, quantitative outcome metrics, strategic inter-agency partnerships, and proposed funding framework for sustainable clinical expansion.

A Spectral Clustering-Type Algorithm for the Consistent Estimation of the Hurst Distribution in Moderately High Dimensions

Patrice Abry, Gustavo Didier, Oliver Orejola, Herwig Wendt — 2026-10-11 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s13171-026-00462-9

Abstract Scale invariance (fractality) is a prominent feature of the large-scale behavior of many stochastic systems. In this work, we construct a provably convergent and easily interpretable algorithm for the statistical identification of the Hurst distribution (in particular, the scaling exponents) undergirding a high-dimensional fractal system. Starting from recently established properties of wavelet random matrices, the algorithm is based on modified spectral clustering. The algorithm also includes a model selection procedure so as to address the important practical problem of picking the value of the clustering precision hyperparameter. In a moderately high-dimensional regime where the dimension, the sample size and the scale go to infinity, we show that the algorithm consistently estimates the Hurst distribution. Monte Carlo simulations show that the proposed methodology is efficient for realistic sample sizes and outperforms another popular clustering method based on mixed-Gaussian modeling. We apply the algorithm in the analysis of real-world macroeconomic time series to unveil evidence of cointegration.

Sharp Stability-Ambiguity Geometry for Exterior-Power Differentials

Bin Du — 2026-10-11 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23289316

We study the differential of the kth exterior-power map on self-adjoint operators, with particular emphasis on the relation between the full singular spectrum and the diagonal differential Gram matrix. At a diagonal operator we obtain an orthogonal decomposition of the full differential into a diagonal block and one-dimensional off-diagonal modes, and prove that the smallest singular value always comes from the diagonal block. This yields a canonical normalized stability quantity in arbitrary dimension and exterior degree, with a sharp isotropic upper bound. Using the elementary-symmetric invariant loss of the diagonal Gram spectrum, we introduce a scale-free coefficient fiber in the missing kth elementary coordinate and prove that every such nonnegative-root fiber is a closed interval. For k=2 the maximal fiber width is dimension-independent and equals 1/4, giving a canonical ambiguity normalization. We then relate fiber critical points exactly to Gram eigenvalues, obtain an affine-sphere description of the normalized Gram spectrum, and derive sharp stability-ambiguity frontiers in dimensions three, four, and five. The five-dimensional frontier is realized by a doubly multi-active 2+2+1 endpoint structure and strictly dominates all lower-support embedded branches. The results provide a unified spectral and coefficient-fiber framework for stability and inverse ambiguity in exterior-power differentials.

GPNMB interacts with SMAD4 to mediate microglial neuroinflammation and induce demyelination in diabetic cognitive dysfunction

Bingxue Song, Jiahui Li, Yunshuang Zhao, Wenqiang Liu, Yufei Wang — 2026-10-11 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s13578-026-01664-2

Abstract Background Diabetic cognitive dysfunction (DCD) is a prevalent hyperglycaemia-induced central nervous system complication driven by hippocampal neuroinflammation. Glycoprotein non-metastatic melanoma protein B (GPNMB) exerts anti-inflammatory neuroprotection in neurodegenerative diseases, but its role and mechanism in DCD are undefined. SMAD4 restrains NF-κB-mediated microglial inflammation, and the physical and functional GPNMB–SMAD4 interaction in diabetic brain injury has not been explored. This study aimed to uncover GPNMB’s protective effects against DCD and its SMAD4/NF-κB-dependent regulatory pathway. Methods We established streptozotocin (STZ)-induced diabetic C57BL/6J mice and high-glucose (HG)-stimulated primary hippocampal microglia as in vivo and in vitro models. Stereotactic intracerebroventricular injection of adeno-associated virus-GPNMB (AAV-GPNMB) upregulated hippocampal GPNMB, with KGN (SMAD4 agonist) and A83-01 (SMAD4 inhibitor) utilized for rescue assays. Cognitive performance was evaluated via the Morris water maze. Immunofluorescence and immunohistochemistry detected microglial morphology, GPNMB colocalization and myelin integrity. Western blot and qPCR measured levels of GPNMB, SMAD4, p-NF-κB, M1/M2 markers and inflammatory cytokines. Co-immunoprecipitation (Co-IP) and molecular docking confirmed the GPNMB–SMAD4 protein interaction. Statistical comparisons were conducted using Student’s t-test and one-way ANOVA with Tukey’s post-hoc test. Results GPNMB mRNA and protein were downregulated in diabetic mouse hippocampi and HG-treated microglia. AAV-mediated GPNMB overexpression rescued cognitive impairment, inhibited excessive microglial M1 polarization, normalized inflammatory cytokine profiles, and restored myelin MBP/CNPase levels in vivo and in vitro. Bioinformatic prediction, molecular docking, Co-IP and immunofluorescence validated direct GPNMB-SMAD4 binding. Hyperglycemia depleted SMAD4 and triggered NF-κB activation, whereas SMAD4 stimulation suppressed NF-κB-driven neuroinflammation. SMAD4 inhibition by A83-01 fully eliminated GPNMB’s neuroprotective, anti-inflammatory and anti-demyelinating benefits. Conclusion Hyperglycemia suppresses microglial GPNMB, disrupts GPNMB-SMAD4 binding, and de-represses NF-κB signaling. This drives M1 microglial polarization, neuroinflammation, hippocampal demyelination and cognitive deficits. GPNMB overexpression mitigates DCD via SMAD4 stabilization and NF-κB suppression. The GPNMB/SMAD4/NF-κB cascade is a viable therapeutic target for DCD.

Exploring transcriptomic and genomic latent variable correction approaches in differential expression analysis

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Yadusayan Appulingam, John Jammal, Aminah Ali, Simon Topp, Hemali P. Phatnani — 2026-10-11 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-69619-8

Abstract Differential expression analysis is a central tool for studying the biological processes altered in human diseases via transcriptomic signatures. However, transcriptomic datasets are systematically confounded by latent variables from two distinct sources: unmeasured technical and biological heterogeneity within the expression data, and expression differences driven by population stratification. Correction using expression-based surrogate variables (SVs) and genotype-based principal components (PCs) addresses these sources independently, yet no study has directly evaluated their combined use against either method alone within a differential expression framework. In this study we hypothesised that simultaneously including both correction layers would produce more biologically valid and reproducible results than either approach alone, and tested this in two independent post-mortem RNA-seq datasets of amyotrophic lateral sclerosis (ALS) cases and controls with paired genotype data. Four nested differential expression models (corrected for PC-only, SV-only, both SV and PC, and neither PCs nor SVs) were evaluated across the KCLBB (96 cases and 52 controls) and ALS Consortium (272 cases and 35 controls) datasets. Models were evaluated on cross-dataset effect size concordance, cross-dataset replicability quantified by the Jaccard Similarity Index, and biological recall against a curated reference set of 66 known ALS genes. The combined SV + PC framework outperformed simpler models across most metrics. Replicability improved nearly ten-fold compared to the non-corrected model, (Jaccard index: 2.28% to 19.5%), and the combined framework exhibited a statistically significant 2.2% gain over the SV-only model. Biological recall doubled compared to SV correction alone. Effect size magnitude consistency was preserved, though a reduction in Spearman’s rank correlation indicates PC correction refines magnitude rather than gene rankings. These findings remained generally robust to genotype PC number and sequencing platform adjustment, with more variable performance observed under ancestry restriction and upon extension to an independent tissue. This study found that SVs and genotype PCs address non-redundant sources of confounding, and we recommend their combined use as standard practice in differential expression analysis where paired genotype data are available, with the greatest benefit expected in ancestrally diverse cohorts. Notably PCs capturing population structure can also be derived directly from RNA-seq data, potentially extending this framework’s applicability to studies lacking paired genotype data. Although this analysis was restricted to ALS datasets, we expect these findings to generalise to other traits.

Hardware-Assisted Intrusion Detection System for Automotive Embedded ECUs

Vishnupriya.T, Sumit Ramswami Punam — 2026-10-11 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.31838/esa/03.02.04

Modern automotive systems progressively rely on Electronic Control Units (ECUs) to take control of vital vehicle functionality and are heavily interlinked on in-vehicle networks, e.g. the Controller Area Network (CAN). With the increased vehicle sophistication into a complex cyber-physical system, the car is more exposed to cyber-related attacks such as message injection, spoofing, and denial-of-service (DoS) attacks that have the potential to affect safety and performance. Implementations of Intrusion Detection Systems (IDS) using software, can be not very effective when it comes to real-time detection, because of the computational overhead imposed by them and the shared CPU usage. The paper proposes a new hardware-assisted intrusion detection system (H-IDS) designed to work on the automotive platforms and embedded devices (ECUs) in particular but is focused on the low-latency and real-time nature of the system and its capability to deploy on resource-constrained devices. The described architecture uses a light on-chip co-processor in the ECU as a special security engine to observe CAN traffic and observe abnormal behavior based on temporal and statistical characteristics. The H-IDS performs detection tasks on a separate processor-offloading payload so it does not interfere with other processes or even stop when the vehicle does not perform its tasks. This was implemented on an ECU platform based on the ARM Cortex-R5 in a hardware prototyping system with FPGA and then tested in a range of simulated attack conditions. The input of the experiment shows that there is a significant reduction in the detection latency that the H-IDS displays an average latency of 14 microseconds that is much faster than software-only solutions. Also, the design has less than 2 percent CPU overhead and its power and memory requirements fit squarely within the power and memory budget of most automotive embedded systems. The provided proposed H-IDS is aligned with ISO/SAE 21434 standards of cybersecurity, and it is scalable to be deployed on various heterogeneous ECU systems. Through the increased detection responsiveness and negligible resource consumption, the work is a step towards realistic hardware-enforced security measures, which can be incorporated in the next generation of automotive architectures to guarantee resilient and trustful in-vehicle networks.

Understanding molecular interactions between Dengue virus capsid protein (DENV-C) and cellular lipid droplets (LDs): A SANS study

Professor Wuge Briscoe, Zeyuan Zhuang, Mr Guangzhen Wang, Miss Holly Stockdale, Dr Peixun Li — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5286/isis.e.rb2610600

We have performed trial SANS and XRR measurements in response to helpful comments from reviewres (Fig.2 and Fig.3). Dengue virus (DENV) is the cause of mosquito-borne dengue fever. Endemic in 100 countries, it leads to over 400 million infections and 25k deaths annually worldwide. With no effective treatments or vaccines, it represents a major socio-economic burden for tropical and subtropical developing countries. Expansion of dengue Aedes mosquito vector due to climate change makes it increasingly a global threat, with over 4 billion people at risk, including EU and UK. There is an urgent need for detailed understanding of the DENV lifecycle for tackling DENV infections. Central to DENV s lifecycle is its replication, a process that has been very recently linked with lipid droplets (LD; ubiquitous multifunctional intracellular organelles of 0.05-100 um in size), with DENV capsid proteins (DENV-C) found accumulating on LD, leading to nucleocapsid formation and viral particle self-assembly. Critically, this process is initiated by the adsorption of DENV-C onto the LD surface. As part of a recently funded EPSRC (UK) SFI (Ireland) project to understand such molecular interactions, here we propose a SANS study of artificial lipid droplets (ALDs) and their interactions with DENV-C proteins, correlating the protein sequence with the ALD-peptide complex nanostructure. The results will underpin future vaccine development strategies exploiting suppressing DENV-C adsorption on LDs.

Surveillance, Detection and Therapeutic Innovation in Antimicrobial Resistance in the Age of Polycrisis: From Genomic and Digital Innovation to One Health Integration

Francesco Branda, Francesco De Maria, Ivailo Alexiev, Ayşe Banu Demir, Önder Limon — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3390/microorganisms14102308

Antimicrobial resistance (AMR) surveillance is increasingly strained by interconnected global crises, i.e., pandemics, climate change, armed conflicts, population mobility, and health inequalities, that disrupt healthcare delivery, diagnostic capacity, and laboratory infrastructure. In parallel, artificial intelligence (AI), machine learning, whole-genome sequencing, metagenomics, and digital surveillance offer the potential to shift from reactive reporting to predictive intelligence that anticipates resistance threats, although current implementations remain sector-specific, retrospective, and project-funded. Environmental surveillance, particularly wastewater monitoring coupled with genomic epidemiology, provides early warnings at population and ecosystem levels. The future integration of AI across human, animal, and environmental sectors could enable unified One Health intelligence; no such integrated platform is currently operational. This review focuses primarily on bacterial AMR. It examines how global drivers strain surveillance, how genomic, metagenomic, digital, and AI-enabled technologies reshape AMR detection and prediction, and the emerging non-antibiotic therapeutic landscape, including bacteriophage therapy, Clustered Regularly Interspaced Short Palindromic Repeats (CRISPR)-based antimicrobials, antimicrobial peptides, microbiota-directed interventions, nanomedicine, host-directed therapies, photodynamic therapy, and vaccines. It then explores how these innovations can be integrated within a One Health framework to support coordinated global preparedness. Addressing the AMR crisis will require not only technological innovation but also equitable implementation, interdisciplinary collaboration, and resilient systems.

Parental Knowledge, Attitudes, and Practices Toward Influenza Vaccine Uptake Among Children in Jeddah, Saudi Arabia: A Cross-Sectional Study

Amjad Alharazi, Majid Alghamdi — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64819/art000020

Background: Seasonal influenza is a major public health concern, particularly among children who are at high risk of infection and can transmit the virus to others. Vaccination is the primary preventive measure. Parental knowledge, attitudes, and practices (KAP) may influence vaccination decisions. Methods: A cross-sectional study was conducted among Saudi parents attending primary healthcare centers in Jeddah. Parents with at least one child aged six months or older were eligible. Data were collected using a validated self-administered Arabic questionnaire assessing knowledge, attitudes, awareness, perceived barriers, and vaccine uptake. Descriptive statistics and chi-square tests were used to examine associations, and binary logistic regression was performed to identify independent predictors of vaccine uptake. Results: A total of 308 parents participated. Influenza vaccine uptake was reported by 57.5% of participants. Nearly all participants (97.7%) demonstrated good knowledge, but the knowledge score was not significantly associated with vaccine uptake (p = 0.399). Parental attitude was a significant independent predictor (OR = 1.090, 95% CI: 1.033-1.149, p = 0.001). Education level was also significantly associated with uptake (p = 0.015), with lower-educated parents showing higher odds of vaccination compared to postgraduate-educated parents. Exposure to awareness campaigns was significantly associated with higher uptake (p < 0.001). The most reported barriers were the belief that vaccination was unnecessary (9.4%) and concerns about side effects (8.4%), both strongly associated with non-vaccination (p < 0.001). Conclusion: Although most parents had good knowledge about influenza vaccination, this did not translate into consistent vaccination behavior. Parental attitudes and awareness campaign exposure were the main predictors of vaccine uptake. Public health strategies should shift from broad factual dissemination to targeted, point-of-care counseling that addresses misconceptions about vaccine necessity and safety to improve influenza vaccination rates among children in Saudi Arabia.

— 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.25258/ijddt.16.63s.33

Recent advancements in nanotechnology have revolutionized drug delivery systems by enabling targeted and controlled delivery of therapeutic agents.Despite significant progress, challenges such as poor bioavailability, drug toxicity, limited stability, and uncontrolled drug release continue to hinder the effectiveness of conventional drug delivery approaches.Nanosponges have emerged as a promising nanocarrier system capable of overcoming these limitations due to their unique structural and functional characteristics.Nanosponges are nanosized, three-dimensional porous structures composed primarily of cross-linked cyclodextrins and suitable cross-linking agents.The nanosponge drug delivery system offers numerous advantages, including reduced drug toxicity, prolonged and predictable drug release, enhanced therapeutic efficacy, and site-specific drug targeting.In addition to pharmaceutical applications, nanosponges have demonstrated significant potential in the delivery of proteins, enzymes, vaccines, antibodies, and other biomacromolecules.Their applications extend to various therapeutic areas, including cancer treatment, autoimmune disorders, infectious diseases, and theranostic medicine.This review provides a comprehensive overview of nanosponges, focusing on their preparation methods, factors influencing formulation, physicochemical characterization techniques, advantages and limitations, and diverse pharmaceutical applications.Furthermore, recent developments and future perspectives in nanosponge-based drug delivery systems are discussed.The growing body of evidence suggests that nanosponges represent a versatile and innovative platform with immense potential to enhance the efficacy and safety of modern drug delivery systems.

A modular constitutive single-plasmid platform for efficient production of a recombinant Strep A glycoconjugate vaccine

Mark A. Harrison, Tom Flood, Mark Reglinski, Mark McNeil, Helge C. Dorfmueller — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s42003-026-11158-z

Abstract Production of glycoconjugate vaccines has long relied upon chemical methods which are highly complex and expensive, resulting in vaccines that are not widely affordable. Heterologous bioconjugation methods, such as Protein-Glycan Coupling Technology (PGCT), have emerged as a path to significantly cheaper manufacture using safe host organisms, such as Escherichia coli , for recombinant glycoconjugate expression. PGCT glycoconjugate synthesis components are typically encoded across three plasmids and require the addition of exogenous chemical inducers for gene expression. In this study, we utilise modular DNA assembly to generate a single constitutive plasmid for biosynthesis of a Streptococcus pyogenes rhamnose polysaccharide glycoconjugate vaccine. This approach resulted in a greater than seven-fold glycoconjugate yield as well as simplified cell culture conditions compared to the traditional three plasmid inducible system. Importantly, given the modular nature of this method, this innovation could be applied to the manufacture of glycoconjugate vaccines against a range of pathogens.

Toxoplasma gondii Infection: From Environmental Transmission and Host–parasite Interactions to Clinical Disease, Diagnosis, and Translational Opportunities

Zainab W. Kermasha — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.9734/ijtdh/2026/v47i101784

Toxoplasma gondii is an obligate intracellular apicomplexan that links environmental contamination, food-animal production, wildlife ecology, maternal-fetal health, and opportunistic disease. The outcome of infection is determined by interactions among parasite developmental stage and genotype, host immune competence, tissue context, route and magnitude of exposure, and the parasite’s ability to reprogramme host-cell biology. This review integrates environmental transmission with molecular pathogenesis, immunity, persistence, clinical disease, diagnosis, and translational opportunities. Felids shed environmentally resistant oocysts that contaminate soil, water, and food, whereas tissue cysts maintain foodborne transmission. After invasion, tachyzoites form a non-fusogenic parasitophorous vacuole and inject rhoptry and dense-granule effectors that modulate host transcription, interferon-mediated restriction, cell death, migration, and nutrient acquisition. While IL-12/IFN-γ-driven cellular responses define protective immunity, differences between murine and human cell-autonomous immunity limit direct extrapolation from experimental models. Maintenance of chronic infection is biologically active rather than inert and involves persistence and reactivation through the BFD1-BFD2 differentiation circuit (Waldman et al., 2020; Licon et al., 2023), tissue-cyst architecture (Blader et al., 2015; Sullivan & Jeffers, 2012; Weiss & Kim, 2013; Eberhard et al., 2025), metabolic remodelling (Wang et al., 2026), and ongoing immune surveillance (Matta et al., 2021; Eberhard et al., 2025). Clinically, disease ranges from asymptomatic infection to congenital disease, ocular toxoplasmosis, encephalitis, and disseminated disease in immunocompromised hosts. Serology remains the main diagnostic tool, but determining when infection occurred is difficult because IgM may persist and assays vary. Molecular assays provide direct evidence of parasite DNA, but their performance is highly dependent on specimen type, parasite load, extraction procedure, and target. Current therapies effectively suppress replicating tachyzoites but do not reliably clear tissue cysts, which remains a major translational bottleneck in chronic-stage biology. Standardised diagnostics, human-relevant experimental systems, stage-specific therapeutics, multistage vaccines, integrated parasite-host omics, and One Health surveillance linking human cases with livestock, felids, wildlife, food, water, and environmental contamination will be required for future advances.

Vaccine recipient and volunteer experiences from COVID-19 mass vaccine clinics at a university campus: drive-through and walk-through approaches

Joana Tome, Jessica S. Schwind, Holly L. Richmond, Brian Deloach, Deborah Harvey — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.vaccine.2026.129229

Purpose This cross-sectional study characterized the implementation and differences between drive-through (DTCs) and walk-through vaccination clinics (WTCs) during a 2021 mass vaccination effort at Georgia Southern (GS) University to inform future clinic designs. Methods Students, faculty, and staff participated as vaccine recipients or volunteers during the event. Pearson's Chi-square, Fisher's Exact, and qualitative methods (line-by-line open coding/focused and axial coding) compared DTC and WTC experiences collected through surveys. Crude and adjusted ordinal regression models assessed vaccination experience across clinics. Results 1294 COVID-19 vaccine recipients' and 222 volunteers' experiences were recorded. High vaccination satisfaction levels were observed in both clinics (91.79% (DTCs) vs. 95.17% (WTCs)), and no significant differences were observed in the vaccination satisfaction experience (aOR: 1.16 (0.60–2.31)). However, WTCs operated faster than DTCs, showcasing a shorter total median waiting time [30 min (IQR: 5–75) vs. 45 min (IQR: 1–180)]. High marks were observed in the volunteer training adequacy, job sheet accuracy, and equipment availability. In the qualitative analysis, a positive experience with well-organized clinics was observed in both clinic types. Conclusions With no significant differences recorded between clinics, future mass vaccination strategies should focus on optimizing site set-up, streamlining service delivery, and enhancing the overall recipient experience across all clinic types.

Immunization with an Escherichia coli-Produced Hepatitis E Virus Genotype 3 Virus-like Particle (HEV-3_p495) Confers Protection Against HEV-3 and HEV-4 Challenge in Pigs

Satoshi Kunita, Masaharu Takahashi, Tominari Kobayashi, Kazumoto Murata, Hiroaki Okamoto — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3390/v18101122

Hepatitis E virus (HEV) genotypes 3 and 4 are major zoonotic pathogens, with domestic pigs serving as an important reservoir. Vaccination of pigs may therefore help reduce HEV circulation and zoonotic transmission. We evaluated the protective efficacy of an Escherichia coli-produced HEV-3 virus-like particle (VLP), HEV-3_p495, against homologous HEV-3 and heterologous HEV-4 challenge in pigs. Juvenile pigs vaccinated three times with either 80 or 400 μg of HEV-3_p495 remained negative for HEV RNA in serum and feces during prolonged cohabitation with naturally HEV-3-infected pigs, whereas all non-vaccinated controls became infected. At market age, vaccination also provided strong protection against intravenous HEV-3 challenge, with complete protection against detectable infection in the four-dose group and in two of three pigs in the three-dose group. Importantly, HEV-3_p495 conferred cross-protection against HEV-4 following cohabitation exposure. Two-dose vaccination prevented detectable infection in two of three young pigs and delayed fecal virus detection without viremia in the remaining pig. At market age, all vaccinated pigs remained negative for HEV RNA in serum and feces following HEV-4 exposure, whereas all non-vaccinated controls became infected. These findings provide proof-of-concept evidence for homologous and cross-genotype protection by HEV-3_p495 and support its further evaluation as a candidate swine vaccine for reducing HEV infection and shedding.

Mitochondrial vulnerability in mRNA vaccine-associated myocarditis: an incomplete framework that overlooks primary endothelial targeting

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Hartmut H. Glossmann — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23279300

Mori et al. in Nat Commun (2026). doi:10.1038/s41467-026-71295-1 propose that mitochondrial vulnerability underlies mRNA vaccine-associated myocarditis through reactive oxygen species-mediated necroptosis in cardiomyocytes. While their clinical observations and mouse model represent valuable contributions, the mechanistic framework contains a critical internal contradiction: the authors demonstrate that empty lipid nanoparticles cause cardiac dysfunction yet simultaneously conclude from a methodologically inadequate biodistribution analysis that lipid nanoparticles do not reach the heart. Published evidence directly contradicts this conclusion. We propose that an endothelium-first model, in which lipid nanoparticles preferentially target cardiac capillary endothelial cells through unique haemodynamic and anatomical mechanisms, provides a mechanistically complete alternative framework in which the mitochondrial findings of Mori et al. represent downstream rather than primary events.

Vaccine-encoded spike in the nucleus ?

Hartmut H. Glossmann — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23279638

Whether spike protein is present in skin, and where it comes from, has been asked twice: first of infected patients, then of vaccinated ones. Detection in the infection era rested on immunohistochemistry with reagents later shown, by one of the groups that used them, to stain pre-pandemic tissue. Nucleocapsid is actively secreted independently of virion assembly and circulates two orders of magnitude above free spike. Its presence in perfused tissue says nothing about local replication. Endothelial cells lack ACE2 and are not productively infected. This excludes local viral synthesis as a source of endothelial spike. Against this background, several vaccine-era reports share a feature the earlier literature lacks: spike positive with nucleocapsid negative, in patients whose infection has been serologically excluded. The intervals from mRNA injection range from 10 to 1,364 days.

Japanese Encephalitis (JE) remains a significant global public health challenge in Asia, necessitating the development of efficient and scalable vaccine production methods. Traditional vaccine manufacturing often relies on serum-containing media and static culture systems, which pose risks of contamination and limited scalability. Vero cells are widely accepted mammalian cell lines used in research and commercial applications, particularly for vaccine production in both human and veterinary medicine(s). Abstract Tuberculosis (TB), caused by Mycobacterium tuberculosis, reclaimed its status as the deadliest single-pathogen infectious disease globally in 2023, accounting for 10.8 million new cases and 1.25 million deaths in 2023.
