An efficient and versatile reverse genetics system for bovine coronavirus facilitates antiviral evaluation

Yiran Zeng, Hanqiong Gao, Yuanlu Lu, Menglu Fan, Zhiyuan Liu — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s13567-026-01851-6

Abstract Bovine coronavirus (BCoV), a primary etiological agent of calf diarrhea, exhibits dual tropism for the respiratory and enteric tracts. However, research on BCoV gene function and pathogenesis has been limited by the lack of convenient and flexible reverse genetics tools. Herein, we report a robust reverse genetics system for BCoV based on an optimized circular polymerase extension reaction (CPER) methodology. We successfully rescued recombinant BCoV (rBCoV) in HRT-18G cells and engineered recombinant BCoV expressing ZsGreen fluorescent protein (rBCoV/Zs-T2A-ns2). Phenotypic analysis demonstrated that both unmodified rBCoV and rBCoV/Zs-T2A-ns2 maintained growth kinetics comparable to the wild-type BCoV. Importantly, the reporter virus demonstrated remarkable genetic stability for over ten passages, identifying the ns2 locus as a suitable insertion site for stable foreign gene expression without compromising viral fitness or sequence integrity. As a proof-of-concept application, the rBCoV/Zs-T2A-ns2 was employed for antiviral compound evaluation, successfully identifying four compounds—camostat, nafamostat, niclosamide, and nelfinavir—capable of effectively inhibiting BCoV replication. Further time-of-addition assays revealed that nelfinavir and niclosamide targeted the BCoV replication stage, while camostat and nafamostat interfered with BCoV internalization. This versatile CPER-based reverse genetics system provides a critical platform for advanced BCoV pathogenesis research and accelerated development of novel vaccines and effective antiviral therapeutics, ultimately contributing to enhanced bovine health and sustainable livestock production.

A Disease-Memory Guided Framework for Radiology Report Generation from Multi-View Chest X-rays

Prajakta Dhamanskar, Chintan Bhupeshbhai Thacker, Saurabh A Shah — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.35882/jeeemi.v8i4.1882

Chest X-rays are widely used, low-cost diagnostic tools. Writing chest X-ray reports manually is a time-consuming and tedious task. The increasing number of X-rays burdens radiologists, creating a need for a clinically accurate, fast and automated report generation system. To solve this challenge, we propose a new disease-memory-guided vision-language framework for radiology report generation. Frontal and lateral chest X-rays (multi-view) are encoded using a Swin-Transformer. These encoded visual features are then passed through a multi-label classification head that predicts disease probabilities, from which the top three labels are converted into learnable disease-memory embeddings. These are concatenated with projected visual tokens and fixed task instruction embeddings to form a multimodal prompt which is fed into a frozen decoder. A LLaMA-2-7B model adapted to the radiology domain using LoRA fine-tuning on the train-only MIMIC-CXR subset is used as an autoregressive decoder. The disease-memory embeddings serve as disease guidance during report generation. An extensive experimental analysis of different experimental variants is conducted on the benchmark Indiana University Chest X-ray dataset using three aspects: 1) statistical n-gram overlap, 2) semantic similarity, and 3) clinical label-based metrics. Experimental results of the proposed system show improvements in semantic evaluation metrics, with BERTScore precision, recall, and F1 values of 0.898, 0.891 and 0.894, respectively, and ROUGE-L and METEOR scores of 0.327 and 0.281, indicating improved contextual consistency between generated and reference reports. However, performance on statistical metrics (e.g., BLEU4=0.068) and clinical evaluation metrics remains limited, with Micro-F1, Macro-F1, Precision and Recall values of 0.4211, 0.0640, 0.4272, and 0.4151, respectively, indicating only partial abnormality description. This framework improves abnormality grounding while maintaining computational efficiency and can be used as a supportive tool for preliminary report drafting in research or resource-constrained settings.

Experimental and statistical investigation of MgO ‐assisted microalgae biodiesel–hydrogen dual‐fuel combustion in an RCCI engine

Korukolu Ratna Raj, A. Saravanan — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1002/ep.70738

Abstract In Reactivity‐Controlled Compression Ignition (RCCI) operation, hydrogen was inducted through the intake manifold as a low‐reactivity fuel (LRF), whereas microalgae biodiesel‐diesel blends enriched with magnesium oxide (MgO) nanoparticles were directly injected into the combustion chamber as high‐reactivity fuel (HRF). This study explored a unique tri‐fuel combination consisting of microalgae biodiesel, magnesium oxide nanoparticles (25 and 50 ppm), and hydrogen (5 and 10 L/min). The experimental investigation was conducted at varying loads with different blends to evaluate engine performance, combustion characteristics, and emission characteristics. The results showed that the blend B20D80 + 50 ppm MgO + 10 lpm H 2 achieved the highest brake thermal efficiency, increasing from 29.5% to 33.8%, represented as 14.58% increase, along with a decrease in brake‐specific energy consumption from 6373 kJ/kWh to 6027 kJ/kWh, corresponding to a 5.43% reduction compared with diesel. The improved combustion performance can be attributed to the enhanced oxidation of biodiesel, the catalytic activity of MgO, and the high flame speed of hydrogen, leading to notable reductions in hydrocarbons (HC), carbon monoxide (CO), carbon dioxide (CO 2 ), and soot emissions. However, NO x emissions increased due to the higher in‐cylinder temperatures. Combustion analysis further indicated increased in‐cylinder peak pressure and heat release rate, from 65 to 75 bar and 63 to 74 J/°CA, respectively, indicating a faster and more efficient combustion process under hydrogen‐assisted RCCI operation. Overall, the proposed tri‐fuel approach demonstrated enhanced engine efficiency, albeit with a trade‐off in terms of increased NO x emissions and potential long‐term engine durability concerns.

Holographic Complexity as a Probe of Boundary Entropy in AdS/BCFT

Fabiano F. Santos — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3390/sym18101684

We investigate how the sewing operations familiar from topological quantum field theory provide a useful organizational framework for semiclassical AdS/BCFT path integrals. Our purpose is not to identify AdS/BCFT with a topological field theory: the bulk geometry, the end-of-the-world brane, and the on-shell action remain intrinsically metric-dependent. Rather, we use the TQFT language of state preparation, orientation reversal, and gluing to separate interface data from local geometric contributions. For AdS3/BCFT2, we review how the Euclidean on-shell action and the Ryu–Takayanagi prescription reproduce the Affleck–Ludwig boundary entropy logg after holographic renormalization. We then formulate the Complexity=Action calculation in the presence of an end-of-the-world brane, carefully distinguishing bulk, brane, joint, and null-boundary contributions. We emphasize that finite terms in a relative Wheeler–DeWitt action depend on the choice of regulator matching and on the null-boundary counterterm scheme unless their scheme independence is established explicitly. Finally, we extend the geometric construction to a planar BTZ background and derive the brane embedding from the Neumann boundary condition. The resulting framework clarifies which aspects of holographic complexity are sensitive to boundary conditions and which require further renormalization input before they can be interpreted as universal boundary data.

A Machine Learning Framework for Mobile Money Fraud Detection Using Gaussian Naïve Bayes: A Transaction-Level Evaluation on the Synthetic Paysim Dataset

Davies Isobo Nelson, Abiodun Ibrahim Akintunde — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.47191/ijmcr/v14i10.02

In Mobile money services are increasingly targeted by fraud, including SIM-related abuse such as SIM swapping, which undermines user trust, exposes sensitive financial data, and costs telecom operators and financial providers billions of dollars annually. Traditional rule-based detection systems have proven insufficient for identifying evolving fraud patterns, motivating the adoption of machine learning approaches. This study presents the design and implementation of a transaction-level mobile-money fraud screening system built around the Gaussian Naïve Bayes (GNB) classifier and evaluated on the synthetic PaySim dataset. The system was implemented following the Rapid Application Development (RAD) methodology using object-oriented design techniques. The dataset was split 80%/20% into training and held-out test sets; normalization was fitted on the training data, and the Synthetic Minority Oversampling Technique (SMOTE, sampling ratio 0.1) was applied to the training set only, leaving the test set at its natural class distribution. Furthermore, predicted fraud probabilities were converted to class labels using a decision threshold of 0.3 instead of the conventional 0.5. The developed model achieved 99.91% accuracy, 89.22% precision, 88.67% recall, 88.94% F1-score, 99.95% specificity, an AUC-ROC of 0.9425, and a false positive rate of 0.05%. The findings indicate that Gaussian Naïve Bayes provides an interpretable and computationally practical baseline for transaction-level fraud screening. However, becuase PaySim contains no SIM-specific signals such as SIM-swap events, device/IMSI changes, or OTP request patterns, the developed model should be understood as a general mobile-money transaction fraud screen rather than a dedicated SIM-fraud detector; the synthetic nature of the dataset and the conditional-independence assumption of Naïve Bayes further limit direct generalization to live telecommunications environments. Future work should incorporate real-world telecom and mobile-money data with genuine SIM-level features, adaptive fraud behaviour, deep learning or sequential models, and real-time stream processing.

Reply on RC2

Run Liu — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5194/egusphere-2026-2846-ac2

Abstract. Surface ozone (O3) concentrations in China have continued to increase despite substantial reductions in primary air pollutants, but the relative roles of meteorological variability and longer-term non-meteorological changes remain uncertain across seasons and regions. Here, we applied a LightGBM-based meteorological normalization framework combined with SHapley Additive exPlanations (SHAP) to investigate interannual variations in maximum daily 8 h average O3 (MDA8 O3) across China during warm and cold seasons from 2015 to 2024. The model reproduced daily MDA8 O3 variability reasonably well, with mean testing R2 values of 0.71 and 0.77 in the warm and cold seasons, respectively. Observed national mean MDA8 O3 increased by 2.1 μg m-3 yr-1 in the warm season and 1.7 μg m-3 yr-1 in the cold season. After meteorological normalization, MDA8 O3 still increased at 1.2 μg m-3 yr-1 in both seasons, indicating that the decadal O3 increase was mainly associated with non-meteorological components. SHAP analysis revealed distinct seasonal and regional meteorological associations. During the warm season, temperature and solar radiation were more important in northern inland and basin regions, whereas relative humidity and wind-field variables were more important in southern coastal regions. During the cold season, solar radiation dominated across most regions, while relative humidity was more important in the Pearl River Delta. The net meteorological contribution shifted from generally negative during 2015–2019 to positive after 2019, indicating that the recent unfavorable meteorological conditions have amplified O3 pollution in several regions.

The Thirteenth Thought Experiment: Why Does a Particle That Has Acquired Mass Not Dissipate and Vanish? - Coherence as a Relation, Harmonic Exchange of Localized Waves, Internal Clocks and Particle Lifetimes Considered from a Single Interaction

Noriaki Kihara — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23266250

This thought experiment begins with a naive question about classifying wave-packet collapse and decoherence as separate phenomena. Using experience with laser holography as a guide, it asks whether coherence is not an absolute attribute of a single wave but depends on the partner wave, the harmonic structures of both, their phases and the readout conditions. A localized wave built from aligned high harmonics forms a sharp peak, but its spatiotemporal alignment with another localized wave is extremely restrictive. When a localized wave scatters on a monochromatic background wave, its high harmonics can be redistributed to the background. If a particle acquires a mass-like response through interaction with the background, why does it not keep losing harmonics and dissipate? Re-examining the author's earlier closed two-wave scattering experiment shows that localization is not lost in one direction but is redistributed back and forth between the two waves. The original program was re-run as a byte-identical copy (all data and figures identical to the original output): the two channels are orthogonal, so the per-collision normalization is a no-op and the update is the pure exchange operator U_R; no dissipation occurs, each wave keeps unit power while its peak height changes by a factor of about 30, and the effective harmonic orders satisfy N_A = 1 + 31 sin^2(k omega/2) with N_A + N_B = 33 exactly. If such circulation closes stably, finite periodicity U^n = I may be observed as a result, not imposed as a rule. The paper asks whether periodic closure, phase locking, quasi-stationarity and lifetimes appear as results, and whether the temporal and spatial readouts of the circulation correspond to internal clock delay and Lorentz contraction. The 'background' is treated not as a container with places, times and a number of waves, but as the anonymous part of the total state not yet distinguished by interaction readouts; the paper ends by asking whether particle species are names for conserved quantities of the interaction. This is a thought experiment that formulates verification tasks arising from the reinterpretation of existing numerical experiments; it does not complete derivations of mass, the Lorentz metric, quantum energy levels or real particle lifetimes. Main text in Japanese with an English translation; the verification package of the re-run is included as a zip archive.

Si-scRNA-seq: unlocking stable single-cell transcriptomics under am-bient conditions

谢广婷(Guangting Xie) — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.26036/cnp0008735

Single-cell RNA sequencing faces critical challenges due to RNA instability during storage. We developed spatial-ly confined biosilicification (SCB) and Si-scRNA-seq for ambient-temperature preservation of single-cell tran-scriptomes. SCB precisely controls silica deposition using a silicification inhibitor, preventing excessive crosslink-ing and cellular aggregation. The extracellular silica network provides physical barriers while intracellular RNA-protein-silica complexes confer structural stabilization, enhancing resistance to oxidative and UV damage. Si-scRNA-seq successfully resolved cellular heterogeneity in lung, liver, and ovarian cancers after extended storage. Compatible with mainstream plat-forms at only 0.1 RMB per sample, this method eliminates cold-chain dependence, providing a practical solution for research and clinical applications.

TOP2A Overexpression as a Prognostic Biomarker in High-Grade Meningioma: An Integrated Single-Cell and Bulk Transcriptomic Study

Hong‐Qing Cai, Guanchao Xie, Houjie Liu, Yujia Chen, Huaixu Li — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3390/ijms27208984

While prior single-cell studies have identified proliferative neoplastic subpopulations, the prognostic and therapeutic implications of TOP2A focal copy-number status have not been defined. This study aimed to delineate the cellular architecture of high-grade meningioma and to evaluate the clinical and prognostic significance of TOP2A expression together with its focal copy-number status through integrated single-cell and bulk transcriptomic profiling. Single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq) was integrated with bulk RNA sequencing (bulk RNA-seq) to resolve the cellular landscape of high-grade meningioma in an exploratory cohort, followed by functional subcluster identification, differential expression analysis, and survival analyses. TOP2A was prioritized for multi-level validation: mRNA overexpression was confirmed in an independent bulk cohort, protein overexpression by immunohistochemistry (IHC), and focal gene copy-number status by fluorescence in situ hybridization (FISH). Kaplan–Meier survival analysis was performed to evaluate patient prognosis. Correlations between continuous variables were assessed using Spearman’s rank correlation coefficient. scRNA-seq of an exploratory high-grade cohort revealed a cell-cycle proliferation cluster characterized by high TOP2A expression. In a 160-sample bulk RNA-seq cohort, TOP2A was overexpressed in high-grade and recurrent tumors, and elevated TOP2A mRNA was significantly associated with shorter progression-free survival (PFS). IHC on 154 specimens confirmed TOP2A protein overexpression, and FISH on 27 specimens demonstrated focal TOP2A copy-number gain enriched in high-grade cases. Copy-number gain was concordant with TOP2A mRNA levels. Although TOP2A mRNA and protein overexpression were each associated with shorter PFS, the limited size of the FISH subset precluded an independent survival analysis by copy-number status. This study identifies TOP2A mRNA and protein overexpression as a prognostic biomarker in high-grade meningioma, and shows that focal TOP2A copy-number gain is enriched in high-grade tumors and concordant with TOP2A expression. By integrating transcriptional overexpression with copy-number gain, our findings identify TOP2A as a candidate prognostic biomarker warranting prospective evaluation in high-grade meningioma.

Evolutionary and Functional Conservation of Topologically Associating Domain Boundaries in Rice Genomes

Owen Geyman, Amina Kurbidaeva — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3390/ijms27208979

Traditional evolutionary analysis has relied primarily on one-dimensional (1D) sequencing data, but recent advances in three-dimensional (3D) genomics allow for deeper investigation of how genome architecture shapes evolutionary divergence. In this study, we analyze conserved and species-specific topologically associating domain (TAD) boundaries across multiple Oryza species to uncover both structural and functional evolutionary insights. Here, we characterize boundary conservation at different levels, from highly conserved to species-unique. We apply TCBF, a relatively novel and underutilized tool for TAD boundary conservation analysis, alongside a suite of complementary analyses, including gene ontology, RNA-seq, sequence conservation, and epigenetic profiling. Our approach not only validates canonical evolutionary relationships reflected in sequence data but also novel patterns unique to 3D genome organization. Most notably, our study uncovers strong associations between highly conserved TAD boundaries and RNA metabolism-related genes, as well as other genetic and epigenetic signatures, and a contrasting absence in minimally conserved boundaries. To our knowledge, we created the first phylogenetic reconstruction derived from topological domain data in plants, which demonstrated a strong similarity between 3D and 1D genomic evolutionary relationships. Overall, our work presents an approach that integrates the 3D genome structure into evolutionary genomics, addressing a critical and underexplored dimension of comparative plant genomics.

Spatial multi-omics uncovers an invasive-desmoplastic cellular community with prognostic and therapeutic implications in pancreatic ductal adenocarcinoma

Changhao Liang, Hua Ye, Hui Xu, Chen Fang, Jian‐Ping Lian — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s44307-026-00141-8

Abstract Pancreatic ductal adenocarcinoma (PDAC) is characterized by a dense desmoplastic stroma and profound spatial heterogeneity, yet the multicellular organizational principles governing its tumor microenvironment remain poorly understood. Here, we performed single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq; n = 9) and spatial transcriptomics ( n = 4), integrating 61,234 single cells and 30,430 spots to identify an invasive-frontier myofibroblastic cancer-associated fibroblast (IF-myCAF), which is characterized by elevated expression of ACTA2 , TAGLN , COL11A1 , THBS2 , CTHRC1 , and POSTN . IF-myCAFs were validated in eight independent PDAC cohorts (159 samples with 67,959 CAFs), showing conserved transcriptional programs and representing a major CAF subpopulation across datasets. Spatial cellular community (CC) analysis identified five recurrent CCs that delineated the multicellular organization of PDAC. Notably, the invasive-desmoplastic CC (ID-CC), which localized to the tumor-stroma interface, was defined by a 5.8-fold enrichment of IF-myCAF; within this community, IF-myCAF colocalized with malignant ductal cells and macrophages. We also found that the ID-CC transcriptional signature was independently associated with worse outcome in TCGA-PAAD. Furthermore, ligand-receptor (LR) analysis of 4,009 IF-myCAF-associated LR pairs identified IF-myCAF as the predominant cell population mediating ECM-related intercellular signaling within ID-CC. Structure-based virtual screening against four IF-myCAF-derived ligand-receptor axes mediating intercellular communication within this community (THBS2-CD47, CTHRC1-FZD8, PPIA-BSG/CD147, MIF-CD74) identified five multi-target drug-repurposing candidates, including conivaptan, lonafarnib, rucaparib, desloratadine, and ketotifen. Collectively, this study first identified a novel CAF subpopulation, IF-myCAF, and established a CC-based organizational scheme for PDAC spatial architecture. Our findings advance our understanding of spatial ecosystem-level stromal heterogeneity in PDAC and suggest targeting IF-myCAF-driven signaling within ID-CC.

FTO-mediated m6A modification of EGR1 mRNA promotes podocyte injury and inflammation via STING1 in diabetic kidney disease

Jinxiu Hu, Yue Liu, Yating Lang, Bing Liu, Xiaoting Fan — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s00018-026-06474-w

Diabetic kidney disease (DKD) is the predominant cause of end-stage kidney disease, with podocyte injury being a key factor in DKD. Research has shown that RNA demethylase fat-mass and obesity-associated protein (FTO) is related to podocyte injury. In prior research, we modulated FTO in podocytes and conducted MeRIP-seq and RNA-seq, through which early growth response 1 (EGR1) was identified. Nonetheless, the precise role of EGR1 in DKD remains to be elucidated. Here, we demonstrated that FTO was critical in podocyte injury, enhancing EGR1 mRNA stability in an m6A-YTHDF2 dependent manner. EGR1 was elevated in podocytes from individuals with DKD, and associated with eGFR and 24 h urinary protein. In vitro , high glucose stimulated EGR1 expression in cultured human podocytes. Silencing EGR1 mitigated the injury and inflammation in podocytes caused by high glucose, whereas its overexpression exacerbated the damage. Mechanistically, EGR1 binds to the STING1 promoter to enhance its transcription, which in turn activates the STING1/TBK1/NF-κB signaling cascade, thereby driving podocyte inflammatory injury. In vivo , podocyte-specific deletion of EGR1 significantly attenuated podocyte and glomerular damage in streptozotocin-induced diabetic mice fed a high-fat diet. Conversely, podocyte-specific EGR1 upregulation worsened podocyte damage and proteinuria in these diabetic mice. In summary, EGR1 played a vital role in DKD podocyte injury, and could be a prospective new treatment strategy for DKD.

RiboFlow v2: a configurable ribosome profiling pipeline reveals measurements sensitive to read-mapping methodology

Daniel Nguyen, Can Cenik — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.10.05.756038

Ribosome profiling experiments capture a snapshot of translation by sequencing ribosome-protected fragments (RPFs). Alignment to a well-curated transcriptome captures most RPF signal, but can miss ribosome activity when expressed exons are absent from the reference. Genome alignment of RPFs expands the detectable sequence space, but can expose ambiguity between homologous loci. Existing workflows often couple reference choice with the handling of ambiguously mapped reads, making it difficult to isolate the effects of either decision on downstream analyses. Here, we developed RiboFlow v2 by adding genome alignment and configurable handling of multimapping reads to the existing transcriptome workflow. Using 24 human ribosome profiling libraries and their matched RNA-seq data, we found that nucleotide-level coverage at matched coding-sequence positions and translation efficiency (TE) were broadly concordant between alignment strategies under stringent alignment filters. Even under these conditions, individual genes showed substantial differences in coverage and TE estimates. These differences included coverage in alternative exons absent from selected transcripts and ambiguous alignments between protein-coding genes and processed pseudogenes. Clustering genes by read-assignment patterns identified groups associated with pseudogene prevalence and highlighted shared exonic sequence among reference transcripts as a source of alignment discrepancies. RiboFlow v2 extends the analysis beyond selected reference transcripts and enables identification of genes whose ribosome coverage and abundance estimates depend on reference composition and how ambiguously mapped reads are handled.

Computational models for biomarker identification in amyotrophic lateral sclerosis using transcriptomics and artificial intelligence

Renu Yadav, P Sriram Kumar, Pragya Pragya, Jac Fredo Agastinose Ronickom — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s44163-026-02415-5

Amyotrophic lateral sclerosis (ALS) is a progressive neurodegenerative disorder characterized by the progressive loss of motor neurons. It is caused by multiple factors, and understanding its molecular pathogenesis remains challenging, particularly in the early stages for identifying diagnostic biomarkers and effective treatments. In this study, we aimed to identify novel biomarkers in ALS and elucidate their molecular mechanisms using transcriptomics and artificial intelligence approaches. We considered two RNA-seq datasets of ALS, namely GSE277709 and GSE234297, obtained from the NCBI GEO database. Initially, the data were preprocessed, which included quality control and filtering. Common genes (12,281 genes) between the datasets were identified and merged into a unified dataset comprising 216 samples. Batch effects were corrected using ComBat-seq and validated through principal component analysis and ANOVA-based $$R^2$$ analysis. We applied machine learning (ML) models including logistic regression (LR), support vector machine (SVM), random forest (RF), and eXtreme Gradient Boosting (XGBoost) to identify differentially expressed genes (DEGs). The identified biomarkers were further validated using DESeq2 log $$_2$$ fold change with a threshold of 0.6 and p value $$< 0.05$$ . Finally, functional enrichment analysis was performed on the identified DEGs using Enrichr tools. Our results demonstrated that batch correction significantly reduced batch-driven separation, with principal component variance decreasing from 70.2 to 19.9% before and after correction, respectively. Furthermore, ANOVA-based $$R^2$$ analysis confirmed an 86.65% reduction in batch-associated variance. We identified a total of 29 coding genes and 38 non-coding genes (67 genes) between DESeq2 and ML models. However, few genes are common between ML models and we received a total of 41 genes (18 coding genes and 22 non-coding genes, and gene symbols were not available for one Ensembl IDs). Our ML models such as LR, SVM, RF, and XGBoost, achieved balanced accuracies of 77.46%, 80%, 77%, and 75.82%, respectively. The enrichment analysis revealed that 10 DEGs were associated with 34 ALS-related biological pathways. Notably, CCND1 and MYL9 was found to be involved in GO, KEGG and Reactome pathways. Overall, our results demonstrate that computational models combining transcriptomics and artificial intelligence can effectively support biomarker identification and pathway discovery in ALS.

Point-Process Modelling of Cell-Type Interaction Structure in CosMx Glioma Data for Hypothesis-Driven Tumor Inference from scRNA-seq

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Felipe Pimenta Carcanholo, Murilo Henrique Anzolini Cassiano, Enzo Marques Panepucci, Márcio Poletti Laurini, Tathiane Maistro Malta — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.10.02.756019

Glioblastoma (GBM) exhibits marked spatial heterogeneity that is lost after tissue dissociation for single–cell RNA sequencing. Here, we developed a spatial–statistical framework to characterize tumor–microenvironment organization and derive molecularly predictable spatial phenotypes from CosMx data. Eight GBM specimens comprising 2,427,362 cells, 15 cell populations, and four malignant states were analyzed, revealing substantial inter–patient differences in cellular composition and density. Fine finite–element meshes best preserved continuous spatial intensity and supported whole–tissue Log–Gaussian Cox Process modelling. Immune populations, including macrophages, microglia, monocytes, cDCs, neutrophils, and T cells, showed spatial attraction toward malignant states. These models generated cell–level measures of conditional intensity and tumor–proximity probability. Transcript abundances and metabolic pathway scores served as the sole predictive features to model these spatial phenotypes. Transcriptomic representations outperformed metabolic pathway scores. Leave–one–patient–out validation reduced predictive performance, and patient–wise harmonization failed to restore cross–patient transportability. Spatial outcome distributions varied between specimens, indicating that molecular–spatial relationships are strongly conditioned by patient-specific tumor architecture. Predicted interaction scores stratified transcriptional subclusters within cell types in external non–spatial single-cell datasets. This framework links spatial point–process phenotypes to molecular states and provides a basis for inferring spatial context from non–spatial single–cell data. Generalizing these models requires larger and more diverse spatial cohorts.

All input data are publicly available from GEO (accessions listed in the manuscript's Supplementary Table S1). See README.md inside the archive for the run order and known Windows-specific notes (including the pure-R CellChat v1.6.1 installation patch).

Metabolic basis of post-infectious sequelae after Ebola virus disease

Anna Sanford, Nell G. Bond, Samuel Ficenec, Charlotte Österman, Payton Farkas — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1007/s11306-026-02544-z

Abstract Introduction Ebola virus disease (EVD) survivors often present with clinical sequelae after acute disease resolution, called post-Ebola syndrome (PES). Why some survivors develop these sequelae and others do not is poorly defined. Altered metabolism has been noted in acute EVD but has not been studied in PES. Here, we identified distinct metabolic pathways in EVD survivors with PES compared to asymptomatic survivors and potential biomarkers of PES. Objectives The purpose of this study was to determine if EVD survivors with PES demonstrated differential metabolism compared to asymptomatic EVD survivors. Methods Plasma was collected from EVD survivors in Sierra Leone as part of an ongoing cohort study. Untargeted metabolomics (LC-MS/MS) was performed on survivors with and without sequelae as well as household contacts. Univariate, multivariate, biomarker and pathway analyses were performed in MetaboAnalyst. Spearman’s R correlation was performed using JMP. Results We identified differential expression of metabolites involved in multiple metabolic pathways in EVD survivors with PES compared to asymptomatic survivors. These pathways include the tricarboxylic acid cycle as well as amino acid, nucleotide, and short chain fatty acid metabolism. Several of these pathways are associated with immune dysfunction. The identified metabolites have potential use as biomarkers of PES. Conclusion EVD survivors with PES exhibit differing metabolism compared to asymptomatic EVD survivors in several pathways. Other studies have linked these pathways to immune dysfunction and chronic viral infection, however further immunometabolic studies of EVD survivors with PES are needed.

Sex-specific intergenerational epigenetic responses to real-world chemical mixture exposure in an outbred sheep model

Oliver George Hargreaves, Wing Yee Kwong, Andrew Warry, Desmond A. R. Tutt, Vasantha Padmanabhan — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1093/pnasnexus/pgag338

Abstract Establishing whether real-world environmental chemical (EC) exposure can induce heritable epigenetic modifications in large, outbred mammals is key to determining long-term developmental impacts of the human exposome. Using an established biosolids-treated pasture (BS) sheep model, we investigated whether gestational exposure to low-level mixtures of EC induced heritable changes in DNA methylation across three generations of sheep. Reduced-representation bisulfite sequencing of liver, blood, and sperm, combined with a structured, lineage-controlled breeding design, revealed widespread but lineage- and sex-specific differentially methylated loci (DML) in F1 offspring, with detectable alterations evident in F2 and F3 descendants. Although most DML were unique to individual sire lineages, or to a single generation, subsets of loci showed repeated involvement across generations and were associated with altered gene expression in F3 descendants. Sperm from F1 males exhibited reduced methylation at numerous loci and, together with seminal plasma, revealed differential expression of several microRNAs. These effects, however, showed limited persistence in F2 males, indicative of intergenerational rather than fully transgenerational persistence. Collectively, these findings demonstrate that complex, low-level chemical exposures can elicit recurrent, sexually dimorphic epigenetic responses in outbred species, but underscore the challenge of disentangling exposure-induced inheritance from genetically regulated methylation variation.

Multi-Omics Analysis Reveals Hormonal, miRNA-Associated, and NLR-Linked Aphid Defense in Lycium barbarum

Ting Yan, Zikai Zhou, Peng-Fei Shi, Jing-yu Yue, Hao Sang — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3390/plants15203097

Aphid infestation constrains goji berry production, yet its defense architecture remains poorly resolved. We integrated comparative transcriptomics, phytohormone profiling, small RNA sequencing, and genome-wide nucleotide-binding leucine-rich repeat (NLR) analysis to dissect aphid defense in Lycium barbarum (cv. Ningqi-10)and susceptible L. chinense. Aphid bioassays confirmed that L. barbarum imposes a significant fitness cost on aphids, reducing survival, longevity, and fecundity. Transcriptomic comparison revealed fewer but more defense-enriched differentially expressed genes in the resistant accession. Weighted gene co-expression network analysis (WGCNA) identified a stress-associated turquoise module (288 genes, r = 0.981) receptor-like kinases and mitogen-activated protein kinase (MAPK) components, bridging extracellular signals sensing and hormone signaling. Phytohormone profiling showed that salicylic acid (SA) accumulated to 72 h, whereas jasmonic acid (JA) peaked transiently at 24 h, producing an increased SA/JA ratio suggesting a shift to SA-dominated signaling. MiR398a-3p and miR319-3p dynamics coincided with this SA/JA shift, suggesting a possible link to hormone-coordinated regulation and candidate targets for validation. NLR analysis identified 151 candidate genes, including 18 L. barbarum-biased NLRs up-regulation under SA-dominated signaling, implying a link to SA signaling. Collectively, these findings support a multi-layered framework integrating SA signaling, transcriptional reprogramming, and coupled miRNA and NLR responses in aphid defense.

Dependence of mechanical properties on microstructural partitioning driven by non-uniform rare-earth distribution in laser-CMT hybrid welded Mg-Al-Zn alloy

Chao Ma, Yanqiu Zhao, Xiong Zhang, Zhangping Hu, Peng Li — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jma.2026.102324

The insufficient mechanical properties in welded magnesium alloy joints, which are inherently due to weak metallic bonds, pose a considerable challenge that restricts their widespread application in lightweight aircraft structures. This paper focuses on the enhancement of mechanical properties in laser-CMT hybrid-welded joints of a newly developed corrosion-resistant magnesium alloy. The WE43 magnesium alloy filler wire with rare-earth (RE) was innovatively introduced to modify the solidification process and strengthening phase formation. The microstructural partitioning characteristics and mechanical properties driven by non-uniform rare-earth distribution were investigated by comparing the Mg-Al-Zn alloy welded joints with AZ31 filler wire and WE43 filler wire. A systematic investigation was conducted to elucidate the microstructural partitioning characteristics, RE segregation behavior, and associated strengthening mechanisms. Results reveal that RE addition significantly alters the solidification behavior, refining the arc zone average grain size to 14.4 µm (a 53.2% reduction). Furthermore, distinct macroscopic RE segregation—characterized by strip-like bands near the fusion line and plate-like accumulations at the pool bottom—was driven by the preferential precipitation of high-density Al₂(Y, Nd) phases migrating under coupled gravitational settling and Marangoni convection. Under optimized parameters, the ultimate tensile strength peaked at 290 MPa, significantly outperforming the AZ31B-reinforced joint (253 MPa). Quantitative deconvolution demonstrates that while precipitation strengthening dictates the RE-reinforced matrix (∼51.0%), the global yield strength is governed by the “weakest link” RE-rich segregation zone, where precipitate coarsening renders grain boundary strengthening primary (42.6%). Ultimately, these coarse precipitates induce local strain localization, triggering a ductile to mixed-mode quasi-cleavage fracture transition.

Deconvoluting Multilateral Environmental Degradation and Structural Negligence Causation in the Forensic Analysis of Deteriorated Coastal Infrastructure

Mohamed Fayez. Elnagar, Structural Consultant & Certified Forensic Engineering Expert MSc, PMP, MCIArb, MASCE, MACI, M.AISC, M.ASTM, M.FIDIC, PhD — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23287437

The forensic investigation of collapsed or severely compromised coastal infrastructure—such as marine ports, seawalls, and beachfront high-rises—presents a complex challenge in separating natural environmental degradation from human structural negligence. The specific dilemma lies in the deconvolution of overlapping degradation mechanisms, including chloride-induced rebar corrosion, sulfate attack, tidal cyclic loading, and the impacts of sea-level rise, from systemic long-term maintenance neglect by asset managers. Over decades of operation, these physical and chemical attacks weaken the structural concrete continuum. When a failure occurs, forensic engineering models must quantify exactly what percentage of the structural capacity loss was due to preventable maintenance negligence (e.g., failing to repair concrete spalling or seal cracks) versus unpreventable, accelerating climate-induced environmental stressors that exceeded historical code-defined design life parameters. Current analytical models cannot reliably separate these co-dependent variables, allowing negligent property owners to legally claim the failure was an unpreventable "Act of God" caused by extreme coastal weather, thereby stalling judicial justice and shifting massive financial recovery burdens onto the public sector.

Decoupling Initial Kinetic Impact Energy From Post Collapse Material Crushing in Forensic Investigations of High Rise Progressive Collapses

Mohamed Fayez. Elnagar, Structural Consultant & Certified Forensic Engineering Expert MSc, PMP, MCIArb, MASCE, MACI, M.AISC, M.ASTM, M.FIDIC, PhD — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23287458

In the wake of a catastrophic high-rise progressive collapse, forensic engineers are tasked with conducting a highly critical reverse-engineering analysis to locate the exact triggering mechanism. The fundamental physical dilemma centers on the inability to mathematically decouple the kinetic energy signatures of the initial structural impact or failure event from the immense, non-linear material crushing that occurs during the subsequent gravitational collapse. As a skyscraper collapses, thousands of tons of concrete floors plunge onto lower levels, generating massive secondary impact forces, friction, and extreme localized pressures that thoroughly pulverize the core columns, shear walls, and structural connections at the base. Consequently, the physical and microscopic forensic evidence of the original design defect or construction error is buried under a highly corrupted and pulverized debris field. Current numerical simulation software can easily model the forward propagation of a collapse, but reversing the chaotic continuum mechanics of millions of fractured elements to prove whether a specific column failed due to a localized structural overload or was simply crushed by falling debris remains a severe mathematical quagmire. This lack of analytical deconvolution allows contractors and designers to challenge forensic expert testimonies, claiming that the initial failure occurred elsewhere in the structure, thereby creating a profound gap in the judicial arbitration of multi-million-dollar structural engineering liabilities.

Microplastics as Ecological Filters Across Food Systems: From Microbial Selection and Antimicrobial Resistance to Human Health Risks and Mechanisms

Christine C. Gaylarde, Estefan Monteiro da Fonseca — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3390/microplastics5040203

Microplastics are increasingly detected throughout food-production systems, from agricultural soils and irrigation waters to aquaculture environments, food-processing facilities, and final food products. Beyond their role as contaminants and microbial carriers, plastic particles provide persistent surfaces for biofilm development and may selectively shape the microorganisms and functional traits that persist during environmental transport. This review introduces an ecological-filter framework for understanding how microplastics restructure microbial communities across food systems and how these processes may ultimately influence human exposure and health. We examine how polymer properties, conditioning films, weathering, environmental gradients, ecological succession, and colonization history drive plastisphere assembly and microbial turnover, with particular attention to pathogen selection, antimicrobial-resistance determinants, and horizontal gene transfer. We further distinguish three partially overlapping mechanisms through which foodborne microplastics may acquire human-health relevance: particle-mediated effects, arising from gastrointestinal interactions and changes in intestinal conditions and microbiota; chemical-mediated effects, involving compounds associated with or released from particles; and plastisphere-mediated effects, involving environmentally selected microorganisms or functional genes transferred through the food chain. Current evidence strongly supports dietary exposure, selective microbial and resistome restructuring, and several intermediate steps of biological transfer, while direct causal links between environmental plastisphere selection and human disease remain limited. We therefore propose that microplastics in food systems should be considered dynamic ecological filters and relays whose biological significance depends on their ecological history, microbial composition, functional traits, processing history, and interactions with the gastrointestinal environment. Integrating microbial ecology, multi-omics, cultivation, food-chain tracking, and mechanistic health assessment will be essential to determine when microplastic-associated ecological selection translates into meaningful human-health risk.

Multi-omics insights into Citrus essential oils: linking biosynthesis, molecular mechanisms, and applications in food, health, and agriculture

Euis Julaeha, Pramesthi Reitza Navisya Vasall, Fauzan Setiawan, Farah Aprisza Sheelmarevaa, Mohamad Nurzaman — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1080/23311932.2026.2745309

Citrus essential oils (CEOs) are complex mixtures of volatile terpenoids with potential applications in food preservation, health-related products, and sustainable agriculture. However, variations in chemical composition, physicochemical instability, and limited mechanistic evidence constrain their standardization and practical utilization. This review critically examines CEO biosynthesis and applications from a multi-omics perspective, focusing on essential oils derived from species of the genus Citrus. Genomic and transcriptomic studies have identified terpene synthase genes and regulatory networks associated with secretory-cavity development and volatile production, whereas metabolomic analyses have revealed species-, tissue-, developmental-, and environment-dependent variations in CEO composition. Structural and functional studies provide complementary insights into enzyme specificity, although direct proteomic evidence in Citrus remains limited. Available integrative studies predominantly combine transcriptomic and metabolomic data, while comprehensive connections among genomic variation, transcripts, proteins, metabolites, and phenotypes remain insufficiently validated. Machine learning approaches have shown potential for authentication, adulteration detection, chemical composition prediction, and composition–activity modeling, but their reliability is restricted by heterogeneous datasets, overfitting, limited interpretability, and insufficient external validation. Nanoemulsion, encapsulation, and controlled-release systems can improve CEO dispersion, stability, and persistence, although their performance depends strongly on formulation properties and application matrices. Further translation requires attention to sensory effects, toxicity, regulatory compliance, standardization, scalability, and economic feasibility. Future research should prioritize matched multi-omics datasets, quantitative proteomics, standardized analytical and metadata protocols, explainable computational models, and experimental validation to support reliable and scalable CEO applications in food, health, and agriculture.

Inferences drawn, evidence unreported: a survey of feature-independence reporting in 151 papers, with one worked case

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Ali Alshanqeeti — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23276640

Evidence-based practice requires that an inference rest on evidence that is reported and open to examination. An increasing share of published inference rests on multi-feature data: gene signatures, multi-marker panels, multi-omic profiles, polygenic scores, and prediction models built from many correlated variables. Whether those features carry independent information bounds what the result can support, and it is computable in minutes from data the authors already hold. Structured searches across PubMed, Europe PMC, OpenAlex and Crossref, using method, object and meta-research concept blocks over three rounds and yielding 728 unique records after deduplication, returned no prior survey of this reporting practice in any biomedical literature. Two purposive samples were then surveyed: 76 cancer cell-line and drug-response benchmarking papers published 2005 to 2024, and 75 cancer-screening papers across eight modalities published 2012 to 2024. A quantity counted as reported only where the source named it as a redundancy or dimensionality assessment, set a criterion for it, acted on the result, or drew a consequence from it for its own inference. On that rule, none of the 151 papers reported any of five standard diagnostics. Sixteen retrospective post-mortem documents from three oncology programmesnamed neither the concern nor dimensionality as an objective. Nine peer-reviewed 2025 to 2026 papers audited the same way scored 0 of 45. One worked case shows what the omission conceals and what it does not. For a 67-gene prognostic signature in Phase III use, the published classifier carried a metastasis-free survival hazard ratio of 2.10 (95% CI 1.15 to 4.06), while a ten-gene proxy that reproduced its class labels at AUC 0.836 carried 1.24 (0.92 to 1.77). Reproducing a classifier is not preserving its prognostic validity, and effective rank and prognostic information can come apart. The check identifies an open question rather than predicting its answer. No current reporting guideline asks for it.

Nanotechnology-Enabled Strategies for Enhancing Plant Abiotic Stress Tolerance

Yusheng Li, Saddam Hussain, Lichen Wang, Siyu Chen, Wei Wang — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3390/plants15203093

Amid increasingly complex climate scenarios, abiotic stresses such as drought, extreme temperatures, and soil salinization have emerged as primary bottlenecks limiting the sustainable development of modern agriculture. Because of their unique nanoscale effects and physicochemical properties, nanoparticles (NPs) can penetrate plant tissue barriers, offering a novel approach to enhance crop resilience to environmental stresses. This review systematically summarizes the core mechanisms by which NPs enhance plant stress tolerance. NPs primarily mitigate the overaccumulation of reactive oxygen species (ROS) and cellular oxidative stress by modulating antioxidant systems, thereby maintaining the physical integrity of photosynthetic apparatuses and cell membranes. Additionally, NPs regulate phytohormone signaling networks and the transcription of stress-responsive genes, while supporting ion compartmentalization and osmotic homeostasis in complex habitats such as coastal saline environments. Integrating multi-omics analyses, this review discusses plant metabolic responses to NPs and their dose-dependent toxicological effects. Addressing bottlenecks in practical application, we evaluate barriers to field-scale translation, the environmental release and migration of nanomaterials across water, air, and the food supply, and associated detection methods and safety risks, including the specific case of widely used silver nanoparticles. Future research must rely on multidisciplinary integration to optimize targeted delivery networks, premised on well-defined risk-assessment standards and viable cost–benefit balances, ultimately providing a scientific basis for enhancing agricultural climate resilience.

Regulated Cell Death in Reproductive Biology: Mechanisms and Therapeutic Opportunities

Sofoklis Stavros, Efthalia Moustakli, Nikolaos Kathopoulis, Stefanos Dafopoulos, Dimos Sioutis — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3390/cells15201837

Regulated cell death (RCD) has emerged as a fundamental determinant of cellular homeostasis and tissue remodeling, extending far beyond the classical concept of apoptosis. The identification of emerging RCD pathways, including ferroptosis, pyroptosis, necroptosis, cuproptosis, PANoptosis, and autophagy-dependent cell death, has expanded our understanding of reproductive physiology and disease. RCD mechanisms contribute to reproductive homeostasis and have been increasingly implicated in gametogenesis, folliculogenesis, embryonic development, implantation, placentation, and reproductive aging, while their dysregulation has been associated with infertility, recurrent implantation failure, recurrent pregnancy loss, pregnancy complications, endometriosis, polycystic ovary syndrome (PCOS), gynecological malignancies, and male reproductive disorders. This review provides a comprehensive overview of the molecular mechanisms governing RCD across reproductive physiology and disease, emphasizing the complex interplay between oxidative stress (OS), mitochondrial dysfunction, inflammation, immune regulation, metabolic reprogramming, and genetic and epigenetic networks. Particular attention is given to the contribution of emerging cell death pathways to reproductive disorders and their interactions with environmental and lifestyle factors, including endocrine-disrupting chemicals. In addition, current evidence regarding RCD-associated biomarkers, multi-omics approaches, and therapeutic strategies targeting specific cell death pathways is critically evaluated, with particular emphasis on distinguishing associative molecular signatures from functional evidence of RCD execution. By integrating recent advances from basic, translational, and clinical research, this review highlights the context-dependent role of RCD in reproductive biology and disease and identifies current limitations and future opportunities for RCD-informed diagnostics, patient stratification, and targeted therapeutic interventions in reproductive medicine.

Polygenic Risk Scores in Alzheimer’s Disease: A Cross-Scale View from Genes to Brain and Behavior

Efthalia Angelopoulou, Ioannis Stamelos, Stefanos N. Sampatakakis, Sokratis G. Papageorgiou, Nikolaos Scarmeas — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3390/biology15201805

Alzheimer’s disease (AD) is a genetically complex disorder in which inherited susceptibility is distributed across rare high-impact variants, the apolipoprotein E (APOE) locus, and a broad polygenic background of common risk alleles. Polygenic risk scores (PRSs) provide a quantitative framework for summarizing this distributed genetic liability and have become increasingly important tools for risk stratification, cohort enrichment, and biological interpretation. In this review, we synthesize evidence on AD PRSs across multiple biological and clinical scales, moving from genes and molecular pathways to biomarkers, brain structure and function, cognition, behavior, and clinical translation. Genome-wide PRSs capture aggregate inherited risk, whereas pathway-, tissue-, cell-type-, and biomarker-specific PRSs may help clarify the biological routes through which genetic susceptibility is expressed, including amyloid and tau metabolism, lipid homeostasis, endosomal trafficking, immune regulation, glial activation, proteostasis, and mitochondrial quality control. Evidence indicates that AD PRSs are most consistently associated with amyloid-related biomarkers, while associations with tau, neurodegeneration, brain atrophy, functional connectivity, cognitive decline, and neuropsychiatric manifestations are more heterogeneous and often depend on APOE, disease stage, biomarker context, and variable computation methodologies. Although PRSs show promise for research enrichment and mechanistic stratification, their individual-level clinical utility remains limited by modest predictive performance, ancestry-related transferability, calibration challenges, and ethical considerations. Future progress will require multi-ancestry cohorts, integration of common and rare variation, multi-omic annotation, longitudinal modeling, and multimodal prediction frameworks. Ultimately, PRSs are best viewed not as stand-alone diagnostic tools, but as cross-scale instruments for linking inherited susceptibility to the heterogeneous biological and clinical manifestations of AD.

The dual destructive effects of cantharidin on red blood cell structure and function: an analysis from membrane elasticity to the metabolic network

Xiaomin Su, Hua Xu, Ziyan Guo, Guoquan Zhang, Hanshi Gong — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-72950-9

Abstract Cantharidin (CTD), a potent anticancer terpenoid, is clinically limited by its hepatotoxicity and nephrotoxicity, while its impact on the hematopoietic system-particularly on red blood cells (RBCs), remains poorly understood. This study aimed to explore CTD-induced structural and functional changes in RBCs. A series of assays were conducted, including assessments of hemolysis rate, morphological changes, programmed cell death, intracellular reactive oxygen species (ROS), lactate dehydrogenase (LDH) activity, as well as untargeted metabolomics (LC–MS) and proteomics (LC–MS/MS) analyses. Results showed that high-dose CTD (0.8 mM) caused obvious RBCs morphological abnormalities, reduced adhesion and increased intracellular ROS, leading to RBC rupture, LDH release, and ultimately triggering programmed cell death. Multi-omics profiling identified 377 differential metabolites (enriched in ABC transporters and purine metabolism) and 45 differential proteins associated with Hippo and PI3K-Akt pathways. Among these, the PI3K-Akt pathway emerged as the core mechanism underlying CTD-induced RBCs injury. Collectively, this study has accumulated data on CTD-induced morphological and functional disruptions in RBCs, providing some insights for the subsequent evaluation of toxic drugs in the hematopoietic system.

CnidoSite: website source code, curated data and database schema at the time of publication

Jiajie She, Longjun Wu, Changsen Jiang, Qian Pei-Yuan, Jiakai Fan — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.23277321

CnidoSite (https://cnidosite.org) is a multi-omics database for Cnidaria. This record is the snapshot of the site at the time of publication, deposited so that the resource survives the website. It contains the site's own source code (PHP, JavaScript, CSS, HTML, Perl, Python and the Apache configuration), the curated data of modest size, the complete schema of all 1,541 tables, and step-by-step rebuild instructions. The source code is released under the MIT licence; the curated data are released under CC BY 4.0. The sequence and derived data are too large for a Git repository (4,538 files, about 261 GiB). The archive ships a manifest of every one of these files with its size and public URL (manifests/large-files.tsv), a script that fetches the 2,154 publicly available files (scripts/download-large-data/), and the pipelines that regenerate the rest (scripts/pipelines/). The remaining 2,384 files are the intermediate per-species annotation tables that the site does not serve over HTTP; they are not required to rebuild the database.

Metabolic reprogramming in cancer-associated fibroblasts: orchestrating the tumor immune microenvironment and response to immune checkpoint blockade

Luoman Gan, Zhonglong Liu, Liren Cao, Xiaoyan Meng, Xuanhao Liu — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s40164-026-00837-6

Abstract Immune checkpoint blockade (ICB) has reshaped the therapeutic landscape of solid tumors. However, durable clinical benefit remains limited to a subset of patients because of primary or acquired resistance. Beyond tumor-intrinsic factors, the tumor microenvironment (TME) plays a critical role in shaping responsiveness to immunotherapy. As a major stromal component, cancer-associated fibroblasts (CAFs) contribute to immune suppression through dynamic metabolic reprogramming and modulate the spatial organization and functional state of immune cells. Recent advances in single-cell transcriptomics, spatial omics, and metabolomic profiling have provided new insights into the metabolic heterogeneity of CAF subsets and their interactions with immune cells. Metabolic reprogramming not only sustains CAF activation but also remodels the local immune environment through nutrient competition, metabolite accumulation, and coordination with extracellular matrix remodeling. These processes reinforce immunosuppression within the TME and contribute to resistance to ICB. This review integrates emerging multi-omics insights into CAF metabolic heterogeneity, summarizes how metabolic reprogramming shapes tumor immunity, and discusses therapeutic strategies targeting CAF metabolism to enhance immunotherapy efficacy, together with the major challenges facing clinical translation.

Recent advances in UDP-glycosyltransferases from medicinal and edible homologous plants: a catalytic toolkit for natural glycoside biosynthesis

Gexin Liu, Lianghua Xie, Eman M. A. Taha, Abul-Hamd El Sayed MEHANNI, Wei Chen — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1080/10408398.2026.2739132

Medicinal and edible homologous (MEH) plants represent a distinctive and underexploited reservoir of bioactive natural products, yet their potential for sustainable biomanufacturing remains largely untapped owing to the limited understanding of the glycosyltransferases that govern the biosynthesis of their key metabolites. Uridine diphosphate (UDP)-dependent glycosyltransferases (UGTs) play a pivotal role in enhancing the bioavailability, stability, and bioactivity of specialized metabolites in these plants. However, a systematic overview of these functionally characterized UGTs is currently lacking. Here, we present a comprehensive curation of functionally characterized UGTs from MEH plants, organized by their substrate classes. We examine the structural and evolutionary principles underlying UGT catalytic diversification, and discuss integrated discovery platforms encompassing multi-omics mining, heterologous expression, and high-throughput screening. Finally, protein engineering strategies such as rational design and directed evolution are examined for optimizing catalytic properties and industrial applicability. This review serves as a foundational reference and provides a catalytic framework for advancing the sustainable production of high-value natural glycosides, underscoring the significant potential of UGTs in biomanufacturing and functional food development.

Urinary Metabolomics in Bladder Cancer: A Scoping Review of Current Evidence and Translation Implications

Ângela Carapito, Filipa Amaro, Ana Cecília A. Roque, Félix Dias Carvalho, Joana Pinto — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3390/ijms27208983

Bladder cancer (BC) diagnosis and surveillance currently rely largely on invasive cystoscopy, making urine an attractive non-invasive biofluid for discovering metabolic biomarkers, given its direct contact with the bladder urothelium. This review provides a comprehensive and critical synthesis of the literature on urinary metabolomic profiling in BC, summarising metabolites and metabolite panels associated with the disease, their relevant metabolic pathways, and diagnostic performance. A search was conducted in PubMed, complemented by records from reference lists of relevant reviews. After screening, 30 original studies comparing urinary metabolomic profiles between BC patients and cancer-free controls were included, encompassing diverse analytical platforms and statistical approaches. A wide range of urinary metabolites were reported to be altered between BC patients and controls, of which 52 were recurrently identified in at least two independent studies. Increased levels of taurine, valine, lactate, adenosine monophosphate and adipate were among the most consistent metabolic alterations, whereas the levels of citrate, glycerol, creatinine, and several dicarboxylic acids and acylcarnitines were consistently decreased. Other metabolites, such as hippurate, tyrosine, and histidine, showed variable trends in their levels. These alterations may reflect metabolic processes associated with tumour growth, energy reprogramming, lipid metabolism, and oxidative stress adaptation. Most studies also proposed multi-metabolite panels combining metabolites to discriminate BC patients from controls, achieving sensitivity and specificity up to 100% and AUC up to 0.983, while fewer studies explored panels for tumour stage and grade discrimination, with more modest performance. Overall, urinary metabolomics reveals a broad, biologically coherent set of metabolic alterations associated with BC, but methodological heterogeneity and limited independent validation still hinder clinical translation. Larger, harmonised, prospectively validated studies integrating multi-omics and machine learning are needed to establish robust urinary biomarkers for BC.

BioTranslate: reproducibility deposit for "A multi-omics assessment framework for extracellular vesicle biomarker translation"

Piyush Bhanu, Andrew K. Godwin, Diane E. Mahoney, Devin C. Koestler — 2026-10-10 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.21364993

BioTranslate: A Multi-omics Assessment Framework for Extracellular Vesicle Biomarker Translation BioTranslate is a reproducible computational resource developed to assess the translational readiness of extracellular vesicle (EV)-associated molecular biomarkers through systematic integration of published multi-omics evidence. The framework integrates 60 published extracellular vesicle studies across five molecular modalities: proteins, miRNAs, mRNAs, lipids, and DNA methylation. The harmonized resource comprises 70,117 molecule-modality entries generated across 25 analytical platforms. BioTranslate organizes molecular evidence across biological compartments and study contexts, distinguishing significant findings, nonsignificant observations, nondetection, and untested molecules. This framework enables systematic assessment of molecular evidence, analytical detectability, biological conservation, and translational readiness. The protein-centered implementation evaluates 977 proteins using an eight-class translation taxonomy. Of these, 908 classifications are generated through deterministic rules and 69 require documented source adjudication. External validation using an independent serum EV proteomics dataset demonstrates an association between prior evidence and subsequent protein detection. The primary analysis reports an odds ratio of 10.56 (95% CI 7.43–15.01), with a five-proxy adjusted odds ratio of 9.14 (95% CI 6.28–13.31). The deposited resource provides the data, executable code, documentation, provenance records, and analysis outputs supporting the accompanying manuscript. Contents of this release The BioTranslate v1 reproducibility archive contains: Harmonized master datasets for five molecular modalities. Executable Python scripts for protein classification and downstream analyses. Supplementary data tables accompanying the manuscript. Source-provenance documentation, construction rules, and validation records. External detectability and sensitivity-analysis results. Scripts and source data for reproducing the manuscript figures. Documentation, dependencies, and instructions for reproducing the reported analytical results. Reproducibility The release provides executable workflows for reproducing the protein classification framework, quantitative analyses, and publication figures. The archive includes a detailed README, source mapping, and a versioned manifest. Third-party publications and externally maintained reference datasets are not redistributed. Their identifiers, provenance information, and retrieval instructions are provided in the documentation. Version information This deposit corresponds to BioTranslate v1, reproducibility release 1.15, associated with the manuscript A multi-omics assessment framework for extracellular vesicle biomarker translation. The archived implementation represents the reproducible scientific resource described in the manuscript. Subsequent BioTranslate software developments are maintained separately. Web platform BioTranslate is available through its public web interface: https://www.biotranslate.io Associated manuscript Bhanu, P., Koestler, D. C., Mahoney, D. E., and Godwin, A. K. A multi-omics assessment framework for extracellular vesicle biomarker translation. Manuscript submitted for publication.
