Progress on therapeutic mRNA vaccines for cancer

Siyu Wu, Huarong Liao, Wei Zhang — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3389/fonc.2026.1980190

Therapeutic mRNA vaccines are a clinically validated cancer immunotherapy platform using in vitro transcribed mRNA encoding tumor antigens to induce durable anti-tumor immunity. Advances in mRNA engineering, lipid nanoparticle (LNP) delivery, and immunomodulatory combinations have propelled this approach from preclinical models to randomized trials with survival benefit. This review systematically examines vaccine efficacy through an antigen–delivery–immunomodulation tripartite framework. We trace the evolution from shared tumor-associated antigens to personalized neoantigens identified by computational pipelines, discuss ionizable lipid chemistry and LNP formulation for controlled tissue tropism and transfection, and analyze the bidirectional crosstalk between vaccine components and innate immune sensing that shapes adaptive T cell responses. Clinical evidence across melanoma, pancreatic cancer, non-small cell lung cancer, and other solid tumors is critically evaluated, alongside combination strategies—checkpoint blockade, oncolytic virotherapy, and adoptive cell therapy to overcome TME resistance. Persistent challenges include optimal neoantigen selection, standardized immune monitoring, and predictive biomarkers. We conclude that future research should prioritize systematic optimization of the tripartite design through multi-antigen targeting, tissue-specific delivery engineering, and rational combination immunotherapy, aiming to establish mRNA cancer vaccines as a standard-of-care modality.

Leaky protection reveals three patterns in the relative advantages of vaccination strategies across transmission intensity

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Yiwen Xing, Jianyi Zhang, Haoliang Cui, Zijie Yang, Qianning Wang — 2026-10-09 — arxiv

DOI : —

Targeted vaccination often prioritizes individuals who contribute most to transmission. However, this rationale may change under leaky protection, whereby vaccination reduces susceptibility at each exposure rather than fully preventing infection. Here, we use a survey-derived individual-level exposure framework for 2,279 residents to simulate infection dynamics across ranges of transmission intensity and vaccine efficacy. We compare two vaccination strategies: the high-exposure strategy (prioritizing highly exposed individuals) and the oldest-first strategy (prioritizing older individuals). As transmission intensity changes, we identify three patterns in the relative advantages of these two strategies. At low transmission, the high-exposure strategy outperforms the oldest-first strategy in reducing both epidemic peak size and final infection proportion. At intermediate transmission, it lowers epidemic peak size but increases final infection proportion, revealing a trade-off between these metrics. At high transmission, differences between the strategies narrow, and the oldest-first strategy has a slight advantage for both metrics. Overall, our findings highlight the importance of considering vaccine failure mode when evaluating vaccination policies under different transmission dynamics.

Hitting-Time Bounds for Asynchronous Q-Learning

Yang Peng — 2026-10-09 — arxiv

DOI : —

We study the last iterate of standard asynchronous Q-learning from a single trajectory of a finite Markov decision process. The trajectory follows a fixed behavior policy whose state--action chain is irreducible and may be periodic. For discount factor $γ$, write $H=(1-γ)^{-1}$, and let $μ_{\min}$ be the minimum stationary state--action probability and $\mathfrak h_\star$ the largest expected hitting time of the reverse state--action chain. We prove that zero-initialized Q-learning with a constant step size achieves sup-norm error at most $\varepsilon$ with high probability using $\widetilde{O}(H^4/(μ_{\min}\varepsilon^2)+\mathfrak h_\star)$ transitions, for $0<\varepsilon\le1$. The statistical term retains the quartic effective-horizon dependence of standard Q-learning, while initialization cost is additive in $\mathfrak h_\star$. For aperiodic behavior chains, a comparison with mixing time yields a transient term $\widetilde{O}(t_{\mathrm{mix}}/μ_{\min})$. An empirical state--action count rule selects a constant step size for a subsequent training run and attains the same rate without prior knowledge of model parameters, target accuracy, or confidence level. The output is the last training iterate, with both stages included in the sample count. The proof uses two deterministic Bellman envelopes to separate statistical error from fixed-policy initialization error and adapts reverse-time hitting-cost compensation from TD learning to state--action propagation. An exploration lower bound holds for arbitrary predictable step sizes. Together with the existing statistical lower bound and its extension to pilot-selected constant steps, it yields matching mean-square rates up to logarithmic factors on the stated large-sample parameter classes.

Finite-Sample Guarantees for Fully Discrete Conditional Diffusion Models

Qiuyun Zhu — 2026-10-09 — arxiv

DOI : —

We establish finite-sample guarantees for conditional diffusion models learned from data and implemented with finitely many denoising steps. Our approach aligns the score-training objective with the sampling grid, allowing statistical estimation and numerical discretization errors to be analyzed together. Under a smooth conditional density model, we derive high-probability bounds on the generated distribution's error and quantify the required number of score evaluations. For every smoothness index $β>0$, a suitable grid yields averaged conditional Kullback--Leibler (KL) divergence $\widetilde O_p(n^{-2β/(d_x+d_y+2β)})$ relative to the original target with or without early stopping. Both the full and early-stopped samplers attain average target total-variation (TV) error $\widetilde O_p(n^{-β/(d_x+d_y+2β)})$. Here $n$ is the sample size, $d_x,d_y$ are the data and conditioning dimensions, and errors are averaged over the conditioning variable. The total-variation bound matches the rate established in existing conditional distribution-estimation theory, up to logarithmic factors.

Thyroid Nodule Segmentation in Ultrasound Images with Conformal Uncertainty Estimation

Francesco Teodori, Pierpaolo Brutti, Umberto Ferraro Petrillo — 2026-10-09 — arxiv

DOI : —

Thyroid nodules affect a large proportion of the general population. Although most are benign, a non-negligible fraction is malignant. Ultrasound imaging is the primary diagnostic modality, and accurate nodule segmentation is a prerequisite for CAD systems to classify malignancy and assess metastatic risk.\\ In this work, we propose a thyroid nodule segmentation framework that integrates a DeepLabV3+, channel-attention modules and post-hoc conformal prediction, providing both accurate mask and pixel-level uncertainty estimates. We address thyroid ultrasound nodule segmentation as the first stage of a pipeline that also includes malignancy classification and metastatic risk prediction. We adopt DeepLabV3+, whose Atrous Spatial Pyramid Pooling (ASPP) captures multi-scale context, with an EfficientNet-B5 backbone for its accuracy-efficiency trade-off. Additionally, we test three channel-attention modules: Squeeze-and-Excitation, CBAM and Grouped Channel Attention (GCA), paired with two loss formulations (Dice-BCE and Boundary).\\ Since binary masks do not indicate prediction confidence, we wrap the trained model with a split conformal prediction layer that produces pixel-level uncertainty maps with statistical coverage guarantees.\\ We evaluate all configurations on the TN3K public benchmark using a fixed split and directly compare them with state-of-the-art methods. The plain backbone already improves upon previously reported results, with CBAM + Dice-BCE emerging as the best overall configuration.

How Many Significant LISA?: A multiple testing correction

Giuseppe Arbia, Francesca Cardile, Vincenzo Nardelli, Niccolò Salvini — 2026-10-09 — arxiv

DOI : —

In spatial data analysis, global measures of spatial correlation provide useful insights into the general spatial patterns, but fail to capture local variations. Local Indicators of Spatial Association (LISA) address this limitation by identifying local clusters and spatial outliers, offering valuable applications in many fields of research such as public health, urban planning and environmental monitoring, to name only a few. However, the application of LISA tools in hypothesis testing is undermined by the presence of the multiple testing problem which dramatically increases the risk of committing Type I errors especially when dealing with very large datasets. Standard corrections procedure (like, e. g., Bonferroni and Benjamini-Hochberg procedures) fail to consider the spatial dependence among the various LISA tests and, furthermore, they are overly conservative. This paper proposes an adjustment that, by incorporating the notion of the "effective sample size", improves statistical inference balancing false discovery rate control and statistical power.

A functional mixed-effects model for the joint analysis of amplitude and phase variations in function-valued traits

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Yilin Chen, Davide Pigoli, John A. D. Aston, Patrick A. Carter — 2026-10-09 — arxiv

DOI : —

Quantitative genetics is the study of continuous traits and the statistical analysis of phenotypic variation, that is, the observable differences in physical traits among individuals within a population. If family relationships between individuals are known, phenotypic variation can be partitioned into genetic and environmental components. Some traits, such as growth trajectories, can be described as a function of a continuous index and are known as function-valued traits. However, function-valued traits from different individuals often vary not only in their shape (amplitude) but also in the speed at which the biological process develops (phase). In this work, we present and compare two approaches to account for amplitude and phase variations in estimating the additive genetic and environmental components. The first one follows from a conventional framework where warping functions remove phase variations, and then the aligned curves are analysed. The second approach adopts the perspective of looking at the aligned curve and the warping function as a joint functional object. In both cases, we propose a functional mixed-effects framework that uses functional principal components as the data-driven basis for genetically correlated data. The proposed procedure is applied to simulations and a real-case study of Tribolium castaneum growth trajectories.

Why Heavy-Tailed Weight Spectra Predict Model Quality

Joseph Wilson, Chris van der Heide, Liam Hodgkinson, Zhichao Wang, Fred Roosta — 2026-10-09 — arxiv

DOI : —

A solid understanding of the predictive success of deep neural networks (DNNs) remains elusive. For example, while many data-dependent metrics exist that seek to predict model quality of DNNs, these metrics may be expensive to compute for large datasets and/or may be impossible to compute when training datasets are not released. A practical and effective predictor of model quality involves analyzing DNN weight matrices, as prior work has established that heavy-tailed spectral distributions in trained weight matrices can be strongly predictive of model quality. However, such metrics lack a rigorous statistical justification. In this work, we aim to understand why these metrics perform well, from a statistical perspective. To do so, we employ a probabilistic framework to derive the marginal likelihood for a DNN, evaluated using only the final layer weights. Across large-scale convolutional DNNs and LLMs, we find that this marginal likelihood predicts model quality. To understand the role heavy-tailed spectra play in predictive performance, we derive the limiting log-marginal likelihood for spectra that follow the (heavy-tailed) High-Temperature Marchenko-Pastur (HTMP) distribution. We show that this limiting quantity is convex in a heaviness parameter, and we derive an optimal heaviness that empirically predicts over-fitting for large-scale DNNs.

From Imbalance to Evidence: Deep Nonparametric Testing for Complex Data under Severe Sample Imbalance

Guanhao Zhou, Xiufan Yu — 2026-10-09 — arxiv

DOI : —

Comparing distributions under severe sample imbalance requires effectively using abundant majority observations while retaining sensitivity to differences involving the smaller sample. This challenge is particularly relevant for complex, non-Euclidean data, such as graphs, whose structural characteristics call for flexible testing procedures. Classifier two-sample tests (C2STs) provide such flexibility, but existing procedures are primarily designed for balanced or comparably sized samples. In this paper, we study C2STs in a highly imbalanced regime, and show that severe sample imbalance may substantially degrade testing performance. Different from conventional imbalanced learning approaches that treat sample imbalance merely as a nuisance for classifier training, we turn it into a source of repeated testing evidence. Building on this insight, we propose an Enhanced Imbalance-Aware Deep Classifier Two-Sample Test (EIAD-C2ST), a classifier-agnostic framework designed for sample imbalance that accommodates diverse data modalities by tailoring classifiers to data structure. For computationally expensive deep classifiers, we further develop a scalable representation-amortized implementation strategy. Theoretically, we establish rigorous guarantees on asymptotic size and characterize conditions for power consistency, allowing the imbalance ratio to diverge. We demonstrate the empirical gains using simulations and an application to blockchain forensics. The results reveal that the majority-sample surplus, when properly leveraged, can be organized into useful statistical evidence, rather than an obstacle, for distributional comparisons with complex data.

On Eigenvector Statistics of Large-Dimensional Sample Covariance Matrices with Applications

Weiming Li, Zeng Li, Qinwen Wang — 2026-10-09 — arxiv

DOI : —

We study the eigenvector-weighted spectral statistics \[ T_n(\varphi,g)=p^{-1}tr\{\varphi(\boldsymbol{S}_n)g(\boldsymbolΣ)\} \] of large-dimensional sample covariance matrices \(\boldsymbol{S}_n\) with population covariance \(\boldsymbolΣ\). These statistics combine functions of sample eigenvalues with sample--population eigenvector overlaps and arise in statistical losses that depend on both. As the dimension and sample size grow proportionally, we establish a joint central limit theorem for \(\{T_n(\varphi_a,g_a)\}_{a=1}^r\) with multiple analytic test functions and population weights, centered by finite-dimensional deterministic equivalents. A key intermediate step is a joint Gaussian process limit for the weighted resolvent traces \[ m_n^g(z)=p^{-1}tr\{(\boldsymbol{S}_n-z\boldsymbol{I}_p)^{-1}g(\boldsymbolΣ)\}. \] The limiting means and cross-covariances are expressed in terms of the population spectral distribution. We apply these results in two settings: one concerns the variance component of minimum-norm prediction risk, and the other concerns the Frobenius loss of fixed rotationally invariant covariance estimators. For fixed affine shrinkage rules, the loss means, variances, and cross-covariances have explicit formulas in terms of population spectral moments.

sc P rofiterole : Clustering of Single-Cell Proteomic Data Using Graph Contrastive Learning via Spectral Filters

Mustafa Coşkun, Filipa Blasco Tavares Pereira Lopes, Pınar Kubilay Tolunay, Mark R. Chance, Mehmet Koyutürk — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1177/15578666261491608

Novel technologies for the acquisition of protein expression data at the single cell level are emerging rapidly. Although there exists a substantial body of computational algorithms and tools for the analysis of single cell gene expression (scRNAseq) data, tools for even basic tasks such as clustering or cell type identification for single cell proteomic (scProteomics) data are relatively scarce. Adoption of algorithms that have been developed for scRNAseq into scProteomics is challenged by the larger number of drop-outs, missing data, and noise in single cell proteomic data. Graph contrastive learning (GCL) on cell-to-cell similarity graphs derived from single cell protein expression profiles show promise in cell type identification. However, missing edges and noise in the cell-to-cell similarity graph requires careful design of convolution matrices to overcome the imperfections in these graphs. Here, we introduce scProfiterole (Single Cell Proteomics Clustering via Spectral Filters), a computational framework to facilitate effective use of spectral graph filters in GCL-based clustering of single cell proteomic data. Since clustering assumes a homophilic network topology, we consider three types of homophilic filters: (i) random walks, (ii) heat kernels (HK), (iii) beta kernels (BK). Direct implementation of these filters is computationally prohibitive, thus, the filters are either truncated or approximated in practice. To overcome this limitation, scProfiterole uses Arnoldi orthonormalization to implement polynomial interpolations of any given spectral graph filter. Our results on comprehensive single cell proteomic data show that (i) GCL with learnable polynomial coefficients that are carefully initialized improves the effectiveness and robustness of cell type identification, (ii) HK and BK improve clustering performance over adjacency matrices or random walks, and (iii) polynomial interpolation of spectral filters outperforms approximation or truncation. The source code for scProfiterole and Supplementary Data are available at https://github.com/mustafaCoskunAgu/scProfiterole.

Microbial biotransformation of plant-derived bioactive compounds: structural modifications, enhanced bioavailability, and functional food applications

Ying Ni, Wensheng Zhang, Yao Sun, Zhengyong Yu, Youhua Xu — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.26599/jfb.2026.95035452

Abstract Plant-derived bioactive compounds, including polyphenols, polysaccharides, alkaloids, terpenoids/saponins, lignans and glucosinolates, are widely valued for their health-promoting potential, yet poor bioavailability and, in several cases, biological inactivity in native form limit their translation into functional food ingredients. Microbial biotransformation, carried out by naturally occurring gut microbiota, food-fermentation microorganisms and plant-associated endophytic fungi, offers a means of overcoming these limitations through mild, highly selective enzymatic modification. This review synthesizes current evidence on microbial biotransformation across five major classes of plant bioactive compounds, examining the natural microbial sources involved, the core enzymatic mechanisms, including glycosidases, esterases, oxidoreductases, decarboxylases and demethylases, responsible for these conversions, and the resulting changes in bioavailability and bioactivity for each compound class. Beyond the compound-specific discussion, we identify a smaller set of structural modifications, namely deglycosylation, molecular weight reduction, hydroxylation, demethylation, ring cleavage and decarboxylation, that recur across otherwise unrelated compound classes and account for much of the functional benefit associated with microbial processing. We discuss current applications in fermented functional foods and postbiotics, together with the principal obstacles, including strain stability, substrate specificity in complex matrices, and regulatory characterization, that constrain industrial-scale deployment, and outline multi-omics-guided and precision-fermentation strategies likely to shape future progress in this field.

Cyberbullying has become a major problem in social media, which poses severe psychological and emotional dangers to the users, especially adolescents and young adults.The explosive development of Multi-omics profiling uncovers diagnostic biomarker panel, progression-associated subtypes, and prognostic signature in pediatric mitochondrial disease

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Xiaoting Lou, Zhehui Chen, Yuwei Zhou, Keyi Li, Jieyu Feng — 2026-10-09 — openalex

Pediatric mitochondrial disease (PMD) is challenging due to its genetic heterogeneity, diagnostic difficulty, and unpredictable trajectories. Current biomarkers lack sensitivity to diagnose PMD or predict its severe course. Here, we show a multi-omics framework with longitudinal monitoring to resolve these challenges. We identify metabolic and transcriptomic dysregulation in PMD, deriving a robust 7-feature multi-omics diagnostic panel (6 metabolites, 1 transcript). In an independent validation cohort, this panel achieved superior diagnostic accuracy (AUC = 0.96) compared to conventional biomarkers. Here, the authors show that a multi-omics study identified a 7-feature diagnostic panel (AUC = 0.96), revealed two proteomic subtypes with differing outcomes, and developed a 12-transcript prognostic signature (AUC = 0.85).

RNDC-Lab: a real-time calcium imaging-based adaptive neural decoding framework

Kavinda Kehelella, Xiaomin Wu, Hamza Ali, Chen-Wei Yang, Jing Xie — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3389/fnins.2026.1902832

Introduction Real-time neural decoding is critical for precise neuromodulation, which is of increasing interest in the treatment of brain disorders. This paper presents RNDC-Lab, a computational framework for designing, optimizing, and experimenting with real-time, calcium imaging-based, end-to-end neural decoding systems. Methods RNDC-Lab integrates calcium imaging preprocessing with adaptive neural decoding using compact Deep Neural Networks (DNNs). RNDC-Lab supports dynamic retraining of a neural decoding model concurrently with inference, thereby providing robustness to changes in the statistical properties of input calcium imaging signals. We integrate NDSEP and NeuroGRS — two recently- developed tools for neural decoding and signal extraction, and optimized generation of DNN models, respectively—into RNDC-Lab. This results in the first real-time neural decoding system based on calcium imaging with a dynamic, DNN-based decoding model. This paper also introduces a number of simulated datasets to assist in system evaluation and experimentation using RNDC-Lab, and more broadly, as resources for experimentation with arbitrary neural decoding models and systems. The experimental capabilities of RNDC-Lab are further enhanced by four new datasets—the Brain Phantom, N80, N90 and Reward Learning (for multiclass classification) datasets—that we introduce in this work as companion resources for working with RNDC-Lab. Results To demonstrate the capabilities of RNDC-Lab, we conduct extensive experiments to evaluate the functionality and performance of the proposed framework. The experiments incorporate the aforementioned simulated datasets as well as real calcium imaging data collected from mouse models. Our results demonstrate that the RNDC-Lab system, combined with NDSEP and NeuroGRS, achieves efficient and accurate real-time neural decoding from both real and synthetic calcium imaging data. Moreover, the results demonstrate RNDC-Lab's capability for dynamic model retraining and characterize the associated trade-offs involving decoding performance and execution time. Discussion While the components within NDSEP, NGSynth, and NeuroGRS are used to concretely demonstrate the capabilities of RNDC-Lab in this paper, the rigorous dataflow- based architecture of RNDC-Lab enables these components to be selectively replaced with alternative modules that provide the same general functionality using different algorithms or implementations (e.g., a new module for motion correction or model retraining). This makes RNDC-Lab a powerful tool for experimenting with novel aspects of neural decoding in the context of an overall real-time framework.

MORAD: Reinforcement Learning with Multi-Objective Rewards for RNA Inverse Design

Jixin Tang, Ji Guo, Jun Zhao — 2026-10-09 — arxiv

DOI : —

We introduce MORAD, an online reinforcement learning framework for backbone-conditioned RNA diffusion models that trains a single shared policy across diverse target backbones. Structure-conditioned RNA inverse design seeks nucleotide sequences compatible with a target three-dimensional backbone, yet existing reinforcement learning approaches either optimize a separate policy for each target backbone or rely on offline preference optimization, leaving online post-training of a reusable backbone-conditioned model largely unexplored. MORAD combines backbone-conditioned group sampling, group-relative reward normalization, and a forward-process diffusion objective. Beyond existing RL rewards that primarily target tertiary structural quality, MORAD jointly optimizes six measurements spanning tertiary geometry, base pairing, ensemble behavior, and sequence composition. To aggregate these heterogeneous objectives despite differences in scale and optimization direction, MORAD maps each measurement to a bounded desirability score and combines them with a weighted geometric mean. Compared with a tertiary-structure-only reward, this multi-objective formulation improves 10 of the 11 evaluation metrics that have a preferred direction. Applied to pretrained RIDE, MORAD improves GDT-TS by 16.0% (from 0.3300 to 0.3827) and pairing MCC by 19.4% (from 0.6110 to 0.7295), while reducing normalized ensemble defect by 27.5% (from 0.3906 to 0.2830). On the test set, MORAD outperforms RiboDiffusion, gRNAde, and RDesign across tertiary structural accuracy, secondary-structure pairing, and thermodynamic quality, establishing a new state of the art in structure-conditioned RNA inverse design.

In vivo DNA damage protection during cell migration across confining embryonic tissue environments

Hanna-Maria Häkkinen, Soraya Villaseca, Zain Alhashem, Atrin Hamidzadeh, Szymon Chomiczewski — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41556-026-02065-w

Cells migrating through confined spaces can experience nuclear deformation, nuclear envelope damage and DNA lesions, but the physiological consequences of confinement in vivo remain unclear. Using the zebrafish neural crest as a model of developmental migration, we show that tissue confinement increases along the antero-posterior axis and scales with the extent of nuclear deformation. Genetic and mechanical disruption of surrounding tissues relieves confinement and restores nuclear morphology in vivo. Surprisingly, even under extreme deformation, neural crest cells exhibit nucleo-cytoplasmic leakage without nuclear envelope rupture or increased DNA damage. Instead, confinement is associated with reduced LaminB2 at the nuclear envelope. Functional perturbation of LaminB2 reveals that its depletion accelerates recovery from deformation, whereas sustained expression causes persistent nuclear distortion, identifying LaminB2 as a regulator of nuclear deformability. RNA-seq of confined trunk neural crest cells reveals broad upregulation of DNA repair pathways, suggesting that these embryonic stem-like cells are intrinsically resistant to confinement-induced mechanical stress.

Norepinephrine-driven neutrophilic inflammation accelerates ulcerative colitis progression

Shaocong Mo, Kewei Ma, Lingxi Lin, Xuanxuan Pan, Shenyang Zhao — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.jaut.2026.103641

Objectives The role of the sympathetic neurotransmitter norepinephrine (NE) in ulcerative colitis (UC) remains controversial. We aimed to elucidate NE's precise mechanisms in UC, focusing on colonic metabolism, diagnostic potential, and local immune interactions. Methods Colonic biopsies from active UC patients and DSS-colitis mice were profiled. Neurotransmitter levels, including NE, were quantified by targeted metabolomics. An NE-pathway diagnostic model was constructed from bulk RNA-seq data using machine learning. In vivo , NE was administered via enema or intraperitoneal injection at graded concentrations to assess its pro-inflammatory effects. Immune cells were characterized by flow cytometry, scRNA-seq and spatial transcriptomics. In vitro , NE's effects on neutrophils were verified with receptor inhibitors. Clinical relevance was evaluated using pre-treatment mucosal RNA-seq and therapeutic response data. Results UC patients and DSS mice exhibited elevated local NE levels, influenced by increased biosynthesis and reduced degradation. A machine learning classifier based on NE-pathway genes achieved high accuracy (AUC > 0.9) for UC detection. Experimentally, at low concentrations, NE enemas—but not intraperitoneal administration—aggravated acute colitis in DSS mice via colonic neutrophils. Mechanistically, NE exacerbated colitis by enhancing the pro-inflammatory phenotype of neutrophils through adrenergic receptor β2 (Adrb2)-mediated elevation of intracellular ROS. Subsequently, Adrb2 + neutrophils drove inflammatory crosstalk via TNF-TNFR1/2 signaling. Clinically, high ADRB2 + neutrophil infiltration predicted favorable responses to infliximab. Conclusions This study identifies localized NE accumulation as a neutrophil-dependent driver of UC progression via Adrb2-neutrophil-TNF inflammatory cascade, providing a mechanistic rationale that may help guide biologic agent selection and support strategies with selective ADRB2 antagonists in UC treatment.

CD24 blockade reprograms macrophages and synergizes with CD47 blockade to overcome tumor immune evasion

Guocai Tian, You Fu, Hui Zeng, Linjuan Du, Lujun Zhou — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s10020-026-01654-7

Although immunotherapy has revolutionized cancer treatment, only a minority of head and neck squamous cell carcinoma (HNSCC) patients derive clinical benefit, underscoring the urgent need to uncover novel immune evasion mechanisms. CD24 serves as a pivotal innate immune checkpoint that delivers a "don't eat me" signal to macrophages; consequently, CD24 blockade suppresses CD24-high tumors by promoting phagocytosis. However, CD24 is also widely expressed across various immune cell subsets. Whether it drives immune evasion through mechanisms distinct from inhibiting tumor-cell phagocytosis remains largely elusive. This study aimed to investigate the role and underlying mechanisms of CD24 in anti-tumor immunity, focusing on CD24-low HNSCC, and to evaluate its synergistic potential with CD47 blockade. CD24 and CD47 expression profiles in HNSCC were analyzed using The Cancer Genome Atlas (TCGA) datasets and flow cytometry. The anti-tumor therapeutic efficacy of anti-CD24 monoclonal antibodies (mAbs), either alone or combined with anti-CD47 mAbs, was evaluated in subcutaneous (SCC7) and orthotopic (MTCQ1) murine models. To dissect how CD24 blockade reprograms macrophages and enhances antigen presentation, we utilized bulk RNA-seq, single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq), multicolor flow cytometry, immunofluorescence staining, and in vitro co-culture assays with OT-I CD8 + T cells. Furthermore, clodronate-mediated macrophage depletion and antibody-mediated CD8 + T-cell depletion were performed to delineate the hierarchical dependence of the innate and adaptive immune arms. Despite negligible CD24 expression in HNSCC tumor cells, anti-CD24 treatment significantly suppressed HNSCC growth in both orthotopic and subcutaneous tumor models. Mechanistically, CD24 blockade acted as a "molecular switch" to reprogram tumor-associated macrophages (TAMs) from an M2-like phenotype toward an immunostimulatory CD80 + CD86 + MHC-I hi phenotype. This reprogramming enhanced both phagocytic clearance and MHC-I-mediated antigen presentation, which in turn promoted cytotoxic CD8 + T cells activation. Notably, combined anti-CD24 and anti-CD47 treatment synergistically strengthened anti-tumor immunity, triggered a systemic increase in interleukin-2 (IL-2) and interferon-γ (IFN-γ), and potently inhibited HNSCC growth in vivo. These synergistic effects relied on both macrophages and CD8 + T cells. Our findings establish that CD24 blockade activates anti-tumor immunity through a mechanism independent of direct effects on CD24-expressing tumor cells: reprogramming of TAM antigen presentation and consequent CD8 + T cell activation. Dual CD24 and CD47 blockade synergistically suppresses HNSCC growth. By identifying a macrophage-intrinsic mode of action, these findings suggest that CD24 blockade may extend beyond CD24-high tumors to CD24-low disease.

Ferroptosis-related immune microenvironment and prognostic signature in high-grade endometrial carcinoma: integrated bulk and single-cell analysis

Yu Chen, Xidie Li, Yifan Li, Xiaomeng Xia, Xiaoling Fang — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1186/s12957-026-04591-1

Abstract Background High-grade (FIGO Grade 3) uterine corpus endometrial carcinoma (UCEC) is an aggressive malignancy with limited therapeutic options and a poor prognosis. While ferroptosis is implicated in various cancers, its specific role within the tumor immune microenvironment of high-grade UCEC remains unclear. Methods We analyzed transcriptomic and clinical data from 314 Grade 3 UCEC patients in The Cancer Genome Atlas (TCGA). Clustering analysis was used to identify molecular subtypes. Protein-level dysregulation of candidate model genes was corroborated using the CPTAC proteomic cohort. ssGSEA and CIBERSORT algorithms were applied to evaluate immune enrichment across clusters and continuous risk scores, respectively. Single-cell RNA-seq via TISCH2 resolved cell-type-specific marker expression. A robust prognostic model based on seven genes (ANXA1, DDB1, HSDL2, LINC01139, MGST1, PRNP, PTEN) was constructed. It demonstrated strong predictive accuracy, yielding bootstrap-corrected area under the curve (AUC) values of 0.763, 0.844, and 0.882 for 1-, 3-, and 5-year overall survival, respectively. Patients in the high-risk group exhibited a dysregulated immune microenvironment characterized by increased M2 macrophage infiltration. Exploratory in silico pharmacogenomic profiling identified candidate targeted agents with higher predicted drug sensitivity associated with the high-risk transcriptomic signature. Furthermore, single-cell analysis confirmed the overexpression of ANXA1 in tumor-associated immune and malignant cells. Conclusion We developed a novel ferroptosis-related prognostic model specifically tailored for risk stratification in high-grade UCEC. This study links ferroptosis to immune dysregulation and uncovers potential therapeutic vulnerabilities, providing a foundation for future targeted treatment strategies.

IRP1 affects the long-term survival and function of iTregs by regulating ferroptosis and apoptosis

Bo Zhou, Miao Yu, Ziyan Song, Yuying Yang, Yang Sun — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.molimm.2026.10.001

As an important classification of Tregs, iTregs play a critical role in inducing transplant immune tolerance. Iron metabolism is closely linked to iTreg function, and IRP1 serves as a central regulator of cellular iron homeostasis. However, the relationship between IRP1 and iTreg ferroptosis remains unclear. In this study, RNA-seq analysis identified ferroptosis as one of the major pathways altered by IRP1 knockdown in iTregs. Subsequent experiments showed reduced ROS accumulation and lipid peroxidation, together with increased GSH levels and GPX4 expression, indicating attenuated ferroptotic stress. Benchmarking ensemble and deep learning models for drought-responsive gene prioritisation in rice using multi-omics data

Kumanan N. Govaichelvan, Dharini Pathmanathan, Rabiatul‐Adawiah Zainal‐Abidin, Arpah Abu — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0360052

Identifying drought-responsive genes is critical for climate-resilient crop breeding, yet computational gene-trait association often relies on single-omics or generalized genomic datasets. Optimal algorithm selection for high-dimensional, small multi-omics datasets also remains a persistent challenge. We present a machine learning framework that extends the QTG-Finder feature set by integrating transcriptomic and proteomic features to classify drought-responsive genes in rice. Using MBKBase annotations, we evaluated the ability of these models to prioritise 336 drought-responsive genes among 2,416 abiotic stress-associated genes, of which the remaining 2,080 constituted an operational background set. We tested four algorithms: Random Forest, XGBoost, one-dimensional convolutional neural network, and multilayer perceptron, while addressing severe class imbalance and assessing model stability. Tree-based ensemble methods achieved higher performance than the deep learning models in this dataset, which showed early overfitting. The fine-tuned Random Forest achieved a recall of 0.67 and an AUC-ROC of 0.87 under nested cross-validation, conditional on a feature matrix assembled before gene-level data splitting. Across repeated evaluations of a small internal holdout of 10 genes, mean accuracy and recall were 0.90 and 0.63; this set is too small to support a generalizability claim and is reported only as a consistency check. The newly added transcriptomic and proteomic features improved recall from 0.47 to 0.67 compared with genomics alone. Feature importance and ablation analyses indicated that transcriptomics-derived features, particularly measures of expression distribution, magnitude, and direction, contributed most to predictive performance. Functional enrichment analysis supported the biological relevance of the prioritised genes. These results demonstrate the value of multi-omics integration for gene prioritisation in small tabular datasets while highlighting current limitations of deep learning in this setting.

Transcriptomic analysis of zebrafish (Danio rerio) liver exposed to sediments from the Loa River

Nataly Lobos-Parra, Natalia Verónica Leiva, Giovanni Larama, Mariella Rivas, Rodrigo Orrego — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1371/journal.pone.0356064

Metal(loid) contamination in aquatic ecosystems, particularly the Loa River in Chile, poses significant environmental and health challenges. This study investigated the molecular impacts of such contamination by exposing zebrafish (Danio rerio) to sediments from pre-impact and severely impacted zones of the Loa River, utilizing transcriptomic analysis of zebrafish liver. Sediment characterization confirmed arsenic as the main driver of contamination, with As concentrations significantly higher at La Finca. Our findings reveal a clear and distinct cascade of molecular responses in fish exposed to impacted sediments, which provides evidence for an Adverse Outcome Pathway. This AOP commences with Molecular Initiating Events, including significant differential expression of genes related to metal adsorption and homeostasis (slc31a1, slc40a1; FDR-adjusted q < 0.05; p < 0.05 for IT7 vs C0 and IT7 vs PIT7), signifying direct perturbations in intracellular metal regulation. This is followed by Key Events characterized by heightened cellular stress, evidenced by significant upregulation of xbp1, xbp1a, xbp1b, and hsp90a/hspd1 (IT7 vs C0; p < 0.05), consistent with oxidative stress and endoplasmic reticulum dysfunction, as well as hypoxia-like conditions. Progressing further, significant differential expression of angiogenesis-related genes (ptprb, mib1, adam10a, foxc1a; p < 0.05) suggests activation of tissue remodeling processes. The cascade culminates in alterations of genes associated with early carcinogenic signaling, including epigenetic regulation (hdac1) and genomic instability (rassf1, top2b; FDR q < 0.05; p < 0.05). This research demonstrates the power of transcriptomics to differentiate the biological impact of contaminated sediments from pre-impact conditions, offering a detailed molecular fingerprint of sublethal effects crucial for identifying early warning signals of environmental degradation and understanding potential human health impacts.

MYC drives endothelial transformation and dedifferentiation in angiosarcoma

Chen Khuan Wong, Dan Li, Tomer Itkin, Cindy J. Lee, Nicholas A. Teri — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.10.07.757149

MYC amplification is the most common genetic alteration in radiation-associated angiosarcoma and is also present in a subset of de novo primary angiosarcoma, yet its oncogenic role in this vascular malignancy remains unclear. Here, we report that inducible, endothelial-specific MYC overexpression alone was sufficient to drive angiosarcomagenesis in mice. The resulting pulmonary angiosarcoma tumors exhibited impaired endothelial differentiation, indicative of MYC-driven lineage plasticity. Notably, MYC inactivation in dedifferentiated allografts induced growth arrest and restored endothelial gene expression, demonstrating MYC-dependent reversible cellular plasticity. Integrative analyses of MYC ChIP-seq, ATAC-seq, and RNA-seq revealed that overexpressed MYC preferentially occupied highly accessible promoters and activated genes involved in cell growth. Consistent with the murine findings, MYC -amplified human angiosarcomas were enriched for MYC target and cell-cycle programs and depleted of endothelial lineage signatures. MYC overexpression in human endothelial and angiosarcoma cells recapitulated these transcriptional changes, enhanced proliferation, and impaired endothelial tube formation. Together, our results establish MYC as an oncoprotein that promotes endothelial lineage plasticity to drive angiosarcoma initiation and progression.

Identification and validation of biomarkers related to integrated stress response in ischemic stroke based on transcriptomics

Zhou Wei, Jian Chen, Qiuxia Chen, Chaofang Zhou, Rui Xiong — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-75270-0

Abstract Ischemic stroke (IS) is a prevalent cerebrovascular disorder. Integrated stress response (ISR) plays an important role in multiple cerebrovascular diseases, its specific involvement and associated biomarkers in IS remain incompletely understood. This study aimed to identify and validate ISR-related gene (ISR-RG) biomarkers for IS. Transcriptomic datasets related to IS were obtained from public databases, and a curated list of ISR-RGs was derived from the literature. Differentially expressed genes (DEGs) between IS patients and healthy controls were identified through differential expression analysis of the GSE16561 dataset. Candidate genes were then obtained by intersecting DEGs with ISR-RGs. Subsequently, potential biomarkers were pinpointed using machine learning algorithms (LASSO and SVM-RFE), validated by receiver operating characteristic curve analysis, and confirmed by expression consistency across datasets. Correlation analysis among the final biomarkers was performed. A predictive nomogram was developed and its performance assessed via calibration and decision curve analyses. Furthermore, gene set enrichment analysis (GSEA), drug-target prediction, and RNA N6-methyladenosine (m6A) methylation site prediction were conducted to elucidate the underlying molecular mechanisms. Finally, the expression levels of the identified biomarkers were experimentally validated in clinical samples using reverse transcription quantitative PCR (RT-qPCR). Our analysis identified 600 DEGs in the GSE16561 cohort. BNIP3L, G6PD and TAOK1 genes were established as ISR-related biomarkers by integration method. We combined the three biomarkers to analyze the nomogram, and the results showed good prediction accuracy. GSEA showed that all three biomarkers were co-enriched in purine metabolic pathway. Drug predictive analysis identified 43 potential therapeutic compounds for these biomarkers, including imatinib, sodium nitrate and ofloxacin. The prediction of m6A methylation sites showed that TAOK1 had multiple modification sites, and G6PD and BNIP3L had fewer but different modification spectra. Finally, we used RT-qPCR to verify the above markers in clinical samples, which was consistent with the bioinformatics prediction. This study identified three candidate biomarkers (BNIP3L, G6PD and TAOK1) in IS, providing preliminary insights into ISR involvement in ischemic stroke that warrant validation in larger cohorts.

A telomere-to-telomere reference genome for Rosa roxburghii reveals lineage-specific diversification of triterpenoid biosynthesis

Liangqun LI, Mei Peng, Yanfang Yan, Qun Chen, Hong Liao — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s42003-026-11106-x

Abstract Rosa roxburghii ( RRT ) is a nutritionally and medicinally important Rosaceae species that accumulates diverse bioactive compounds, making it an excellent system for investigating the genetic basis and evolutionary diversification of specialized metabolism. Here, we report a complete telomere-to-telomere (T2T), gap-free genome assembly of the elite cultivar ‘Guinong No. 5’ ( RRT5 ), comprising 494.57 Mb with a contig N50 of 51.44 Mb and a base accuracy of over 99.99%. Of this assembly, 99.9% were anchored to seven pseudo-chromosomes. Multi-omics analyses identified OSC , CYP , and UGT genes potentially involved in triterpenoid biosynthesis, while gene–metabolite correlation analyses highlighted MYB30 and MYB31 as candidate regulators of multiple triterpenoids. Notably, the biosynthetic capacities for Kaji-ichigoside F1 and rosamultin vary across lineages within Rosoideae, likely reflecting lineage-specific gene gain or loss and/or evolutionary divergence in regulatory pathways. Together, the RRT5 genome, transcriptome, and profiling of the eight key triterpenoids provide a valuable resource for elucidating triterpenoid biosynthesis, regulation, and evolution, and for guiding genome-informed breeding of R. roxburghii .

SUMO2 deletion changes chromatin accessibility and enhances cytotoxic T cell activation and tumor infiltration

M.D. Neranjan Tharuka, Dai-Hua Chen, Maria Luisa J Amaral, Tianchen Ren, Yixuan Kuang — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1126/sciadv.aef2217

Cytotoxic T cells [cytotoxic lymphocytes (CTLs)] are crucial for adaptive immunity leading to prolonged survival and potential cures for cancer. Recent clinical data have shown that pharmacological inhibition modifies the tumor microenvironment (TME) and activates CTLs, although the mechanism is not well described. In this study, we found that T cell–specific knockout (KO) of the most prevalent SUMO paralog, Sumo2 / SUMO2 , in both mouse and human CD8 + T cells significantly enhanced CD8 + T cell activation without enhanced IFN-I (type I interferon)–responsive genes in vitro but increased chromatin accessibility at enhancer regions for AP-1 (activating protein 1) family members, including BATF and JunB, which are known to promote T cell activation and proliferation. Using antigen-specific OT1 and CAR (chimeric antigen receptor) T cell models, we found that Sumo2 KO CD8 + T cells had significantly higher tumor infiltration, as revealed by flow cytometry, immunofluorescence staining, and single-nucleus RNA sequencing (snRNA-seq), and conferred greater tumor growth inhibition than wild-type control T cells. snRNA-seq also revealed that Sumo2 KO in CD8 + T cells increased the expression of TRAIL (tumor necrosis factor–related apoptosis-inducing ligand) in vivo and activated the antitumor immune microenvironment, likely through cell-cell interaction involving more activated CTLs. These findings elucidate a novel mechanism by which SUMOylation controls CTL activation and tumor infiltration that activate antitumor immunity in the TME. SUMO2 KO can also be a potential strategy to enhance adoptive T cell therapies for solid tumors by enhancing their activation, tumor infiltration and ability to modulate the TME.

Latent-Class Poisson-Lognormal Mixed Models for Clustering Multivariate Longitudinal Count Data

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Pengqi Liu, Utkarsh J. Dang, Sanjeena Subedi — 2026-10-09 — arxiv

DOI : —

Multivariate longitudinal count data arise in biological studies, where responses may be over-dispersed, correlated within subjects and over time, measured at irregular times, and exhibit unobserved subgroup structure. Our goal was to develop a model that can accommodate over-dispersion, longitudinal dependence, cross-outcome dependence, covariate effects, irregular observation times, and covariate-dependent latent-class membership. We propose a latent-class Poisson-lognormal mixed model for clustering heterogeneous multivariate count trajectories. Estimation is performed using a variational EM algorithm designed for scalable inference. Simulation studies show accurate parameter estimation, strong latent-class recovery, and reliable selection of the number of classes, as compared to state-of-the-art approaches. We also applied the proposed approach to longitudinal neutrophil RNA-seq data from hospitalized COVID-19 patients as an illustration. The proposed framework provides a flexible approach for clustering complex longitudinal biological count data. Implementation code is available at https://github.com/PengqiLiu-Mc/latent-class-mpln-mixed-models.

On the convergence of trajectory statistical solutions

Anne C. Bronzi, Cecilia F. Mondaini, Ricardo Rosa — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1088/1361-6544/aeaa9a

Abstract In this work, a recently introduced general framework for trajectory statistical solutions is considered, and the question of convergence of families of such solutions is addressed. Conditions for the convergence are given which rely on natural assumptions related to a priori estimates for the individual solutions of typical approximating problems. The first main result is based on the assumption that the superior limit of suitable families of compact subsets of carriers of the family of trajectory statistical solutions be included in the set of solutions of the limit problem. The second main result is a version of the former in the case in which the approximating family is associated with a well-posed system. These two results are then applied to the inviscid limit of incompressible Navier–Stokes system in two and three spatial dimensions, showing, in particular, the existence of trajectory statistical solutions to the two- and three-dimensional Euler equations, in the context of weak and dissipative solutions, respectively. Another application of the second main result is on the Galerkin approximations of statistical solutions of the three-dimensional Navier–Stokes equations.

Quantum Sampling and Moment Estimation for Transformed Gaussian Random Fields

M. Deiml, Daniel Peterseim — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1137/25m1789834

Abstract. We present a quantum algorithm for efficiently sampling transformed Gaussian random fields in [Formula: see text]-dimensional domains, based on an enhanced version of the classical moving average method. Pointwise transformations enforcing boundedness are essential for using Gaussian random fields in quantum computation and arise naturally, for example, in modeling coefficient fields representing microstructures in partial differential equations. Generating this microstructure from its few statistical parameters directly on the quantum device bypasses the input bottleneck. Our method enables an efficient quantum representation of the resulting random field and prepares a quantum state that approximates it to accuracy [Formula: see text] in time [Formula: see text]. Combined with amplitude estimation and a quantum pseudorandom number generator, this leads to algorithms for estimating linear and nonlinear observables, including mixed and higher-order moments, with total complexity [Formula: see text]. We illustrate the theoretical findings through numerical experiments on simulated quantum hardware.

JESSNet: Joint dEconvolution and Sparse Separation Network for HI 21 cm foreground cleaning

Athanasia Gkogkou, Victor Bonjean, Jean‐Luc Starck, Pauline Gorbatchev, Marta Spinelli — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1051/0004-6361/202662836

i 21 cm intensity mapping, here considered over the redshift range z 0.58, particularly in the presence of a chromatic instrumental beam that couples angular foreground structure into the frequency direction. This problem is further complicated by incomplete sky coverage, as expected for realistic Galactic masks and survey footprints. -- i signal in single-dish intensity mapping observations affected by frequency-dependent beam distortions and masked sky regions. JESSNet advances the joint deconvolution and blind source-separation framework of DecGMCA and SDecGMCA through three methodological extensions. First, while the original formulation uses a single effective mixing model over the angular range being cleaned, JESSNet operates in angular windows, with an effective mixing matrix and a channel selection adapted to each scale. Second, JESSNet replaces the fixed starlet thresholding step with a learned spherical-wavelet ``learnlet'' sparse regularization operator. Third, whereas the original spherical implementation assumes full-sky coverage, JESSNet constrains the source estimates to the observed sky region, making the method applicable to Galactic masks and survey footprints. We tested the method on simulated SKA-MID-like observations covering 900 -- ̊m MHz , including H, i emission, Galactic and extragalactic foregrounds, thermal noise, and an oscillating in frequency MeerKAT-inspired beam model. i signal. Compared to applying JESSNet over a single angular range, the multiscale formulation significantly improves the reconstruction at low and intermediate angular scales and reduces residual beam-induced spectral features. The method also remains effective for a synthetic analysis footprint, preserving the H, i power spectra under incomplete sky coverage. i intensity mapping. The code used to produce the analysis and results has been publicly released, allowing the community to reproduce the pipeline and adapt it to future simulations and real observations.

The circulating lipidome in severe obesity

Mohammad Yaser Anwar, Heather M. Highland, Alexandra B. Palmer, Thy Duong, Zhaotong Lin — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1016/j.isci.2026.117801

Severe obesity (SevO; BMI ≥ 40 kg/m 2 ) is rising globally yet remains understudied mechanistically. To characterize lipidomic alterations at the species level, we analyzed Border Health Research Cohort participants with fasting plasma lipidomics and genetic data, comparing SevO ( n = 185) to non-obese controls (18.5 ≥ BMI < 25; n = 393). We quantified 830 lipid species across 49 classes and assessed associations using logistic regression, orthogonal projections to latent structures discriminant analysis (OPLS-DA), and network deconvolution Mendelian randomization (NDMR). SevO participants showed more adverse cardiometabolic traits. Short-chain triacylglycerols emerged as a distinctive, consistently elevated SevO signature.

Multi-omics transformer integration reveals an invasive-like molecular phenotype in ductal carcinoma in situ

Medha Jha, Yasha Hasija — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1038/s41598-026-74159-2

Abstract Even though 20–30% of cases of ductal carcinoma in situ (DCIS) advance to invasive breast cancer (IBC), cancer histology is unable to accurately differentiate between progressing and indolent disease. An integrated model describing the transcriptomic, epigenetic, and immunological factors influencing this shift has not yet been developed. A hybrid multi-modal transformer comprising gene expression, an expression-derived methylation proxy, pathway scores, and immunological markers was developed using five GEO datasets (n = 415). In 102 IBC/DCIS samples, differential expression analysis revealed 2,996 DEGs, from which a 41-gene core consensus signature was confirmed across two independent validation cohorts. Model performance was tested using five-fold cross-validation, and epigenetic risk was validated using multivariate logistic regression in a separate clinical outcomes cohort (GSE281307, n = 185). The transformer achieved AUC-ROC = 0.9318 ± 0.0132 (overall held-out AUC = 0.8964), sensitivity = 0.8214, specificity = 0.7609, accuracy = 0.7941, and F1 = 0.7917. Conventional baselines trained on the same 75-gene expression matrix achieved numerically higher AUC values (SVM 0.952 ± 0.031, logistic regression 0.942 ± 0.028, random forest 0.944 ± 0.029). However, the transformer’s main benefit is that it produces 512-dimensional multi-modal fused embeddings that distinguish DCIS from IBC much more clearly than the expression features alone (silhouette coefficient 0.986 versus 0.125; permutation p = 0.0005). Additionally, single-modality classifiers are unable to detect a repeatable invasive-like DCIS subgroup. The 41-gene core signature was confirmed by > 80% directional concordance (33 ECM/stromal upregulated; 8 epithelial identity downregulated). Invasive-like samples were identified in 15 out of 46 DCIS samples (32.6%). DCIS development was independently predicted by participation in a high-risk methylation cluster (OR = 2.40, 95% CI 1.27–4.67, p = 0.008). The HER2-enriched subtype was independently predictive (OR = 3.71, 95% CI 1.19–12.51, p = 0.028). ITGA2, SNCA, EN1, COL4A6, COL4A3, and DEFB1 were ranked as the top model predictive features by SHAP analysis on the log-odds scale, with COL4A3 consistently showing up in all model iterations. A new multimodal transformer framework has been developed to identify a subset of invasive-like ductal carcinoma in situ (DCIS), comprising 32.6% of all DCIS samples. This framework uniquely avoids disease-stage label leakage and immunological group-mean leaking, marking the first independent validation of an invasive-like molecular subgroup within DCIS utilising per-sample immune marker expression without target label proxies.

NECTIN4 defines a subtype-dependent tumor state coupling enfortumab vedotin targetability to antitumor immunity in bladder cancer

Kazuki Nishimura, Ryoichi Maenosono, Takuya Owari, Moritoshi Sakamoto, T Nishino — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.10.07.757381

NECTIN4 is the target of enfortumab vedotin (EV), but its biological role in bladder cancer (BC) and its relationship to EV plus PD-1 blockade remain unclear. Integrating clinical multi-omics, spatial and single-cell immune profiling, and experimental models, we identify NECTIN4 as a subtype-dependent determinant of tumor state and antitumor immunity. In basal BC cells, NECTIN4 suppressed inflammatory and mesenchymal programs. Consistently, NECTIN4-high basal/squamous muscle-invasive BCs showed favorable survival, higher checkpoint inhibitor response rates, and a coordinated CD8+ T cell-inflamed microenvironment with no comparable associations in luminal papillary tumors. From Static Material Design to Predictive Open-Loop Control via Chemistry–Architecture Coupling in a Moving-Boundary Reaction–Diffusion Framework

Andualem Belachew Workie — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.26434/chemrxiv.15008964/v2

Bioactive silicate ceramics, wollastonite (CaSiO 3 ), diopside (CaMgSi 2 O 6 ), and apatite-wollastonite glassceramics constitute a unique class of bone-regeneration materials whose therapeutic action is intrinsically coupled to their own degradation.Yet the field continues to design these materials as static objects, optimizing initial composition, porosity and elastic modulus while evaluating outcomes at fixed endpoints, tacitly ignoring the central fact that the physicochemical microenvironment they create evolves continuously and irreversibly.We argue that this temporal evolution is not a side effect but the therapeutic mechanism itself, and that it can be engineered predictively.This work consolidates and mathematically hardens the emerging paradigm of spatiotemporal trajectory engineering: the pre-encoded, open-loop programming of a degrading scaffold's full physicochemical trajectory through coupled control of ceramic network chemistry and pore-network architecture.Three advances over prior treatments are delivered.First, the solid-state chemistry foundation is deepened: Q n speciation from 29 Si MAS-NMR is mapped onto hydration versus hydrolysis activation barriers; the incongruent dissolution cascade (cation exchange → silica-gel layer → hydroxycarbonate apatite nucleation) is formalized as a coupled surface-gel-layer-precipitation problem; and the autocatalytic OH --feedback loop that drives local pH spikes is quantified, together with surface-specific XPS O 1s BO/NBO deconvolution as the front-tracking metric.Second, the static reaction-diffusion model is upgraded to a moving-boundary erosion formulation: an explicit porosity-evolution law, a dynamically self-consistent effective diffusivity, and interstitial convective (Brinkman) transport are introduced, yielding a two-dimensional Damköhler-Péclet regime map that delineates four phenomenologically distinct trajectory regimes, reaction-limited, diffusion-limited, convective washout, and convection-amplified dosing, plus a coupled design corridor where trajectory classes diverge maximally.Third, biological admissibility is replaced from discrete endpoint thresholds to a continuous phase-indexed Regenerative Target Zone (RTZ) envelope incorporating M1-gating, M2-switch, and HCA-maturation kinetics; and the computational pipeline is extended with functional data analysis (fPCA, DTW), neural ordinary differential equations, physicsinformed neural networks, Hamiltonian Monte Carlo credible tubes, and Bayesian-optimal experimental design.The resulting framework is fully specified, falsifiable, and implementable with open-source tools and standard instrumentation.It reclassifies the design problem from optimizing a material to programming an evolving microenvironment, and because the temporal program is pre-encoded without biological sensing, it qualifies as a passive implant under current regulatory frameworks.

Study on tumor ecology and precision medicine strategy of oral squamous cell carcinoma based on multi-omics cell atlas

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Qingkang Meng, Diwei Huo, Fei Xu, Naiqiang Liu, Muchen Fan — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3389/fimmu.2026.1925952

Introduction Oral squamous cell carcinoma (OSCC) is characterized by inter- and intratumoral heterogeneity, which complicates patient stratification and treatment. Although single-cell studies have improved the resolution of the OSCC microenvironment, the relationships among cellular states, spatial organization, immune-repertoire dynamics, and patient-level ecosystem patterns remain incompletely understood. Methods We integrated single-cell RNA sequencing, single-cell T-cell receptor sequencing, spatial transcriptomics, and bulk RNA-sequencing data to characterize OSCC. Results We constructed a multi-omics cellular atlas comprising 88,442 cells and 63 cell subclusters, revealing complementary malignant-cell heterogeneity at the levels of transcriptional subclusters and recurrent meta-programs. Spatial and cell-cell communication analysis identified a reproducible association between PI3+ cancer-associated fibroblasts and APC_Myeloid cells and supported a candidate collagen-associated communication axis within tumor niches. Integration of paired transcriptomic and TCR data delineated a CD8+ T-cell pseudotime continuum accompanied by increasing clonal expansion and exhaustion-associated features, with spatial analysis linking intermediate trajectory states to regulatory T-cell and inflammatory NKT-cell-rich tumor-front regions. Extending the single-cell atlas to bulk cohorts by deconvolution, we defined four OSCC ecotypes and four coordinated cell programs that captured patient-level ecosystem organization and showed distinct molecular and clinical associations. We developed exploratory models for prognosis, recurrence, and immunotherapy sensitivity and established the OSCC Atlas as an online resource for data exploration. Discussion This integrative framework links cellular heterogeneity, spatial organization, immune dynamics, and patient-level ecology, providing testable hypotheses for the biological and clinical stratification of OSCC.

DECONVersation: Single Cell Foundation Model-Derived Embeddings for Robust Cell Type Deconvolution of Bulk RNA-seq

Sélectionné · envoyé par email

Ali Oku, Heather Geiger, Nicolas Robine, Rui Fu — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.64898/2026.10.02.756356

AI and deep learning have transformed single-cell transcriptomics, empowering unsupervised annotation, trajectory inference, and cross-dataset integration at scale. Here, we propose bulk RNA-seq deconvolution as a novel task for single-cell foundation models. Deconvolution estimates cell type proportions from bulk RNA-seq using single-cell reference data, with applications spanning tumor microenvironment profiling to the detection of disease-associated shifts in tissue composition. However, existing methods remain vulnerable to batch effects, subject-level variation, and marker gene selection sensitivity. We introduce DECONVersation, which leverages embeddings from pretrained and fine-tuned single-cell foundation models, coupled with a non-negative least squares solver, to estimate cell type proportions. Foundation model embeddings are robust to batch effects and implicitly encode complex gene relationships, sidestepping explicit marker gene selection. Benchmarked against MuSiC, DWLS, and BayesPrism, DECONVersation achieved comparable or superior performance across diverse tissues. Performance further improved with tissue-specific fine-tuning.

TSC1-mTORC1 signaling in Alzheimer’s disease: an integrated bioinformatic analysis linking gene expression to neuropathology

Zheng Liu, Yuchen Yu, Yu Wang, Shuohan Ning, Tongshuai Huang — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3389/fnagi.2026.1920487

Background and objective Alzheimer’s disease (AD) is characterized by the accumulation of amyloid-β (Aβ) plaques and tau pathology, with mTORC1 hyperactivation implicated in disease pathogenesis via suppression of autophagy. However, systematic evidence regarding its key upstream repressor, TSC1, in human AD brains remains limited. Methods Using an integrated, multi-level bioinformatic approach combining RNA-seq, neuropathological, and clinical data from the ROSMAP cohort, we comprehensively investigated the TSC1-mTORC1 axis in AD. Gene co-expression network analysis and gene set enrichment analysis were applied to contextualize TSC1 within disease-associated molecular networks. Results Our analysis revealed significant TSC1 downregulation and concomitant mTOR and RPTOR upregulation in AD brains. Critically, lower TSC1 expression was strongly correlated with higher Aβ plaque density, greater tau tangle burden, worse global cognitive performance, and an exacerbated neuroinflammatory microenvironment characterized by increased microglial and astrocytic activation. Gene co-expression network analysis positioned TSC1 within a disease-associated module showing the strongest association with AD pathology. Gene set enrichment analyses further demonstrated that the TSC1 low-expression phenotype was associated not only with suppression of autophagy-related pathways but also with significant enrichment of broad pro-inflammatory and immune response pathways. Conclusion and discussion These integrated human data provide compelling evidence that TSC1 deficiency is a hallmark of AD and is associated with mTORC1 hyperactivation, aggravated amyloid-tau pathologies, cognitive impairment, and neuroinflammation. These findings are consistent with a model in which TSC1 deficiency and mTORC1 hyperactivation may contribute to AD pathophysiology, thereby nominating the TSC1-mTORC1 axis and its associated inflammatory networks as promising multi-faceted therapeutic targets. Future replication in independent cohorts is warranted to assess the generalizability of these findings.

Different modalities of time-resolved two-photon fluorescence lifetime measurements for energy transfer analysis of donor-linker-acceptor construct

Gabriela Núñez-Gotia, Isaac Hanauska, Rylee Rosenberg, Malachy Brink, Ezra Olson — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.1117/12.3114472

Förster resonance energy transfer (FRET) is a powerful tool for in vivo and in vitro studies of protein-protein interactions and conformational changes of complex biological molecules. For FRET studies in live cells and tissues, fluorescence lifetime imaging microscopy (FLIM) is advantageous due to the combined spatio-temporal resolution that provides a morphological context for cellular protein-protein interactions. However, these FLIM measurements suffer from some technical and experimental-design compromises to maximize the fluorescence detection efficiency and reduce the exposure time of living cells to pulsed laser (i.e., minimize photodamage) during measurements. For example, the magic-angle fluorescence polarization detection that is essential to rule out the effect of rotational dynamics during the excited-state lifetime is usually ruled out in FLIM experimental design. In addition, the computer-generated instrument response function (IRF) is routinely used for deconvolution data analysis in FLIM measurements for convenience instead of a measured IRF under the same experimental conditions. Here we investigate the FRET efficiency of an ionic-strength biosensor, namely, mCerulean3-linker-mCitrine (or RD), as a model system using different modalities of time-resolved 2P-fluorescence measurements as a function of potassium chloride concentration. We also examined the effects of different aspects of experimental designs (magic-angle polarization detection) and data analysis (IRF, FRET efficiency models) of 2P-FLIM approach as compared with the single-point time-resolved 2P-fluorescence measurements as a benchmark technique. These comparative studies are beneficial to researchers who are using quantitative and non-invasive FLIM for investigating of protein-protein interactions and conformational changes in living cells.

Multi-source data integration and ensemble machine learning analysis of the role of Corilagin in regulating endoplasmic reticulum stress and alleviating intestinal ischemia reperfusion injury in mice

Zhiyang Wang, Qin Han, Lijie Pang, Siyang Zheng, Xin Wang — 2026-10-09 — openalex

DOI : https://doi.org/10.3389/fphar.2026.1966534

Background Intestinal ischemia–reperfusion injury (IIRI) is driven by oxidative stress, excessive inflammation, Endoplasmic Reticulum Stress (ERS), and disruption of the intestinal epithelial barrier. Corilagin, a natural ellagitannin with antioxidant and anti-inflammatory activities, has shown protective effects against IIRI; however, its cross-species and multitarget mechanisms remain unclear. Methods This study investigated the protective mechanisms of Corilagin against IIRI by integrating Corilagin-related targets, IIRI-associated genes, and an ERS gene set. Differential expression analyses of GSE37013 and GSE96733 were combined with an ensemble machine-learning framework comprising 15 algorithms to prioritize core genes. Molecular docking and 100-ns all-atom molecular dynamics simulations using the AlphaFold-predicted full-length human NRF2 structure were performed to assess the predicted Corilagin-NRF2 interaction. A mouse IIRI model was used to validate selected molecular and histological findings by histology, RT-qPCR, Western blotting, and immunohistochemistry. In IEC-6 cells subjected to OGD/R, NRF2 immunofluorescence and siRNA-mediated knockdown of Nrf2 or Abcg2 were used to further evaluate the involvement of NRF2 and ABCG2 in Corilagin-mediated protection. Results Intersection analysis identified 472 genes shared among Corilagin-related targets, IIRI-related genes, and ERS-associated genes. Enrichment analysis highlighted stress- and injury-related pathways, including PI3K-Akt signaling. Ensemble machine learning prioritized 12 core genes across human and mouse datasets, with ABCG2, IL10, and PRKCZ emerging as prominent candidates. Docking and 100-ns MD simulations supported persistent Corilagin retention within the predicted NRF2 binding region, while post-relaxation MM/GBSA analysis indicated favorable interaction energetics. In vivo , Corilagin pretreatment reduced inflammation and oxidative stress-related changes, restored apical ABCG2 and tight-junction protein expression, improved mucosal morphology, and altered CHOP expression. In IEC-6 cells, Nrf2 or Abcg2 knockdown weakened Corilagin-mediated preservation of Occludin, Claudin-1, and ZO-1 under OGD/R, supporting NRF2–ABCG2 involvement in this protective response. Conclusion Corilagin may alleviate IIRI by modulating ERS-related molecular changes and preserving the intestinal epithelial barrier with NRF2 and ABCG2 contributing to a candidate regulatory module involved in this protective response. These findings provide computational and experimental support for further investigation of Corilagin as a potential intervention for IIRI and illustrate the value of combining multi-source data integration with ensemble machine learning to characterize the multitarget pharmacology of natural products.
